## November 18 — The sign-in email limit under concurrency (L-01)

Build plan M2-6a, from the security follow-up approved Nov 17. The limit of five sign-in emails per address an hour counted the address's recent emails and then added one, as two separate steps, so requests arriving together each counted fewer than five and all got through.

| Check | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| The race, shown first | `server/test/api/sign-in-limit.test.ts`, 4 tests, through the API with emails kept in memory. Before the fix, 12 requests at once for one address were all let past the limit (none refused); at two copies of the service on one database, 6 of 12 were refused instead of 7 |
| The fix | `mayEmail` in `server/src/sign-in.ts` counts and adds inside one transaction that first takes a Postgres lock on the address (`pg_advisory_xact_lock`, keyed by the address's hash). Requests for one address take turns, at every copy of the service; other addresses take other locks. The lock ends with the transaction, so a crash can't leave it held. No new package, no migration; addresses stay hashed and the daily job still deletes them after 24 hours |
| After | 12 requests at once, in mixed capitals: exactly 5 let through, 7 refused as too many; the same across two copies, and neither copy sends a sixth afterwards; another address asking meanwhile gets its own five; an hour later the address is sent codes again. Five runs over, all pass. The existing test of the sixth email in an hour still passes |
| Second reader | A fresh agent found nothing to change: the lock's key can't overlap the migration runner's (Postgres keeps one-number and two-number locks apart), it can't deadlock (the holder never asks for a second connection), and Better Auth lowercases addresses exactly as the limit does. One comment claimed other addresses never wait; with a 32-bit key two can share a lock, so it now says they rarely do. A burst for one address can briefly hold that copy's five database connections; each turn takes milliseconds |
| `npm run check` | On Node 22: types, lint, all 711 unit, API, database, and simulation tests (4 new), and all 175 browser tests pass; no saved screenshot changed |
| Known limitation | Requests let through at the same moment for the same address can still collide over the one code an address may have: one is sent, and the others answer with a server error (500) and send nothing. The person still gets one working code. Not part of this item |

## November 18 — Sign-in codes asked for together (L-01)

Found while building M2-6a; the builder chose to fix it next. An address has one sign-in code at a time. Better Auth replaces the old code by trying to add the new one and, on failing, deleting the old and adding again, so requests arriving together for one address could fail twice and answer with a server error (500), sending nothing.

| Check | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| Shown first | A fifth test in `server/test/api/sign-in-limit.test.ts`: 12 requests at once for one address, across two copies of the service. Before the fix, two of the five let past the limit answered 500 |
| The fix | On the two routes that store a code (asking for one; signing in with a wrong one, which puts it back with a try counted), `server/src/sign-in.ts` stores it itself, in one Postgres statement that replaces the address's old code (`insert … on conflict do update`), and Better Auth adds nothing. Of two codes, the one that expires later stays: a new code replaces the old, and a code put back after a wrong try can't replace a newer one sent meanwhile. Other uses of the same table (Google sign-in's state) are untouched. No migration, no package |
| After | All five let past the limit answer 200 and are emailed; the other seven are refused as too many; exactly one of the five codes signs in. Five runs over, all pass. Every existing sign-in test passes, including a new code cancelling the old, the ten-minute expiry, five wrong tries, and Google sign-in |
| Second reader | A fresh agent found nothing to change. It confirmed only the two code routes reach the new step (Google sign-in's state, which needs Better Auth's own insert, never does), that the step stores exactly what Better Auth would have, and that nobody gets more than five tries per code. One comment overstated the rule: two codes that expire at the same millisecond go to the last stored, which it now says. The migration's comment on how Better Auth replaces codes is now out of date and is left alone, since migrations aren't edited |
| `npm run check` | On Node 22: types, lint, all 712 unit, API, database, and simulation tests (1 new), and all 175 browser tests pass; no saved screenshot changed |
| Known limitation | When codes are sent at the same moment, their emails can arrive in either order, and only one code works: the person may type one from an earlier email and need to try the other |

## November 18 — Rate limits (M-12, L-01)

Build plan M2-6, with the limits the builder approved in PR 68 (architecture §7 "Rate limits and abuse"). New feature row M-12.

| Check | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| Compared first (AGENTS.md rule 12) | GitHub's REST API ([rate limits](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/rate-limits-for-the-rest-api)): over a limit, a 429 (or 403) with `Retry-After` in seconds, and wait a minute when it doesn't say. Taken: 429, `Retry-After`, and one minute as the floor. The wording, "Too many in a row. Try again in a minute.", is the build plan's; Google's sign-in says "Too many attempts. Try again later." (from memory), where ours says how long |
| What's counted | Per account, in each instance's memory: new boards 20 an hour; arguments, wordings, edits, and sources added or cited 60 an hour; importance, articulation, and assessments 300 a minute. Per network, asking for a sign-in code to any address: 30 an hour. Reports' 20 an hour is set, for when reports exist (M2-3). Deleting, undoing, Share settings, and reading aren't counted; nor is a request that saves nothing (invalid, or refused by the person's role): it's counted on arriving, so requests sent together can't all slip under, and given back when it saves nothing |
| The network | Deployed (the container), the last `X-Forwarded-For` entry, which Cloud Run appends; locally, the connection's own address. Fastify's `trustProxy: 1` was tried first and, under the tests' injected requests, still answered the connection's address, so it's read by a few lines of our own. An IPv6 address counts by its first 64 bits, the block one line is given. Held only in the counter, never stored or logged |
| Tests first | `server/test/api/rate-limits.test.ts` and `server/test/api/sign-in-network-limit.test.ts`, each limit seen to fail first: the next past each limit is refused with how long to wait and saves nothing, another person or network isn't affected, and it's allowed once the test clock passes the window; a made-up `X-Forwarded-For` can't dodge it, and run locally the header is ignored; a class of 25 on one network, five asking twice, all sign in. `e2e/rate-limits.spec.ts`, 3 tests, seen to fail with the pages' changes set aside: a vote past the limit waits in the board's pill ("Too many in a row. Try again in a minute. · 1 waiting to send") and is sent once the wait has passed; New steelman and Sign in say how long to wait |
| Second reader | A fresh agent found nothing blocking. Fixed: IPv6 counted by address, so one line could take a new address for every request (now by its /64; a test sends 30 from one /64 and the 31st is refused); a misplaced comment and a doubled import; a comment overstating how soon a counter is forgotten; a note in `main.ts` that a proxy in front of Cloud Run would change which entry is the caller's. Added after it: votes made offline and sent together on coming back stop at the limit and the rest wait (a browser test, which passed at once: the queue already did this) |
| `npm run check` | On Node 22: types, lint, all 724 unit, API, database, and simulation tests (12 new), and all 179 browser tests (4 new) pass; no saved screenshot changed. An earlier run timed out in the simulation, which never goes through the limits, while the machine was under heavy load from other programs |
| Known limitations | Counts are per instance (up to three times too generous across three) and start again on each deploy. The board's queue sends in order, so an action waiting on one limit holds back the ones behind it, votes included, for up to the hour the writing limit lasts; sending around it could send a vote before the argument it's on. Reading the network on staging is still to check, since Google doesn't document it. Resend's free plan's 100 emails a day can still run out across instances |

## November 18 — The simulation no longer fails by chance (M-02, M0-5)

PR 71's check (Nov 18, embedded-offline) failed in the simulation, though the pull request changed only documents. Every check held; what failed was the test's demand that each kind of stale action be refused at least once in its 300 random sequences. That run had no action on a deleted argument, which needs a reader to act from an old view on an argument whose last wording has since been deleted. Over the 19 runs before it, that refusal came 3 to 10 times a run.

| Check | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| The fix | `server/test/simulation/world.ts` gives fast-check one fixed sequence to play before the random ones in every run (`EVERY_STALE_REFUSAL`): Ben goes offline and, from his old view, votes on and assesses everything; Cy edits his wording; Ana deletes both of hers, which deletes the argument that had only hers; Ben comes back. fast-check counts that sequence among the runs, so the run count goes up by one: the random sequences are exactly those it played before, and the nine checks are unchanged. The existing test isn't edited; its demand that each refusal happen now rests on the fixed sequence, not on luck |
| Shown first | With 20 random sequences, where the gap is common: 5 runs out of 5 failed as PR 71's did (a refusal missing, "received undefined"); with the fixed sequence, 5 out of 5 passed |
| New test | `server/test/simulation/stale-refusals.test.ts` plays the fixed sequence alone, with all nine checks after each step, and requires an action refused for a deleted argument, a deleted wording, a changed revision, and an assessment without evidence |
| `npm run check` | On Node 22: types, lint, all 733 unit, API, database, and simulation tests (1 new), including both planted-bug tests, and all 181 browser tests pass; no saved screenshot changed |
| Second reader | A fresh agent found nothing blocking, and confirmed the sequence produces each refusal whatever the order on screen (every target is reached, and the offline queue merges none of them). Fixed: fast-check counts the fixed sequence among the runs, so there had been 299 random sequences, not 300 (now one more run); the new test also requires the refusal for an assessment without evidence. The planted skipped-stamp bug is now caught by the fixed sequence, and still shrinks to a one-step example |

## November 20 — The tour (L-02)

Build plan M2-9. The prototype's tour of the three votes (tourSteps, tourTargets, tourNote, and positionTour in `docs/prototype/source/steelman-refined.html`), on the real board's cards.

| Check | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| Compared first (AGENTS.md rule 12), from memory | The prototype settles the design. Figma's and Google Workspace's product tours point at the real interface with a short note, step dots, Back and Next, and a way to skip; GitHub's onboarding notes can be dismissed and don't come back. Taken: nothing beyond the prototype, which already has all of these |
| What it does | On an account's first board with something to point at, the page dims around real cards with a note beside them: importance on the board's top argument ("Vote on how much this argument matters, assuming it's true."), articulation on the argument with the most wordings, its row unfolded ("Vote on which wording states this argument best."), and evidence on the leading wording with the most assessments, framing only its evidence vote ("Vote on whether the linked sources support this specific wording."). A hidden or deleted wording is never an example. Skip tour, Back, Next, and Done; the right and left arrows and Escape. The note is a modal dialog: Tab stays in it, and a click on the board under it does nothing. When it ends, the board is as it was and the cursor is on the question |
| Once per account | `account.tour_seen_at` (migration 0011, additive). The board asks `GET /api/account/tour` and, when the tour opens, says so with `PUT`; asked again, it keeps the first time. ## November 18 — Hide and restore (J-02)

Build plan M2-4, on M2-3's branch (reports), so it waits for that pull request. M2's Done when asks that a report → hide → restore cycle shows in the change log with its reason, and that the simulation includes hide and restore by an editor (Q28).

| Check | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| Compared first (AGENTS.md rule 12) | From memory: Reddit's moderators remove a post with a reason, and it keeps its place as "Removed by moderator"; GitHub hides a comment with a reason ("Spam", "Off-topic", "Abuse") behind "This comment was marked as…", which its maintainers can show. The prototype (removedCard, modItems, reasonMenu) follows them, and is followed here: Hide below the rule in ⋯, "Hide it as" with the report reasons, "Removed by a moderator · Spam" in its place, its text struck through for moderators, and Restore |
| What it does | An Editor or the owner hides someone else's wording, from its menu or from its report flag, with a reason. It keeps a place in its row: where it was, on a board that's open, and after the live wordings on a fresh one (the shared order leaves it out), so a link to it lands there, saying it was removed. Its text, context, earlier wording, and sources reach only Editors and the owner. Restore brings it back for everyone. Each is a change row with who, and a hide with why; hiding resolves the reports open on it, so the flag goes. A vote, assessment, report, or the author's edit on a hidden wording is dropped, as on a deleted one. It leaves Strongest case and the shared order. Nobody is offered Hide on their own wording, as in the prototype; a closed board refuses it, as every write but comments |
| The simulation | Hide and restore are steps: Ana, the owner, may; Ben and Cy are refused. Ana reads as a moderator. A new check, the tenth, after every step: no hidden text reaches anyone but an Editor or the owner, by a request for changes or a fresh snapshot. 1,500 random sequences passed with 291 hides and 34 restores, before the second reader's fixes; after them, the check's 300. `hide-restore.test.ts` plays a fixed sequence (a queued vote on the hidden wording is dropped; a Contributor's hide refused), and shows the tenth check catching a planted leak |
| Tests first | `server/test/api/moderation.test.ts`, 12 tests seen to fail first, then 3 added after the second reader; `role-matrix.test.ts` gains "hide someone else's wording" (Editor and up). Shared: a hidden wording keeps a place, nothing waits for Update, and it leaves Strongest case; web: who is offered Hide or Restore, and no Report on a hidden wording. `e2e/moderation.spec.ts`, 6 tests with two people: hide with a reason, seen by another without its text; restore; hide from the flag; no Hide for a Contributor or on your own; a link to a hidden wording; accessibility scans |
| Second reader | A fresh agent found one blocking problem, fixed: on a board with a hidden wording the pill said "1 change · Update" for good, since the kept place counted as a change and a move (now neither; two shared tests). Also fixed: the author could edit a hidden wording, changing what the moderator reviewed (now refused, and the simulation expects it); "Delete argument" was offered when the only other wording was hidden, though the service keeps the argument; a hidden wording's sources still reached everyone; Restore of a wording deleted since was done; a row led by a hidden wording was named "Deleted by…". Noted, not changed: the flag's menu returns the cursor to ⋯, not the flag; an owner can't restore their own wording from the menu once an Editor hid it (the service allows it); spam on a closed board can't be hidden |
| `npm run check` | On Node 22: types, lint, all 787 unit, API, database, and simulation tests (29 more than M2-3's), and 194 of 195 browser tests (6 more) pass. The one that fails is M2-3's `card-menu.spec.ts` test, left for the builder |
| After the builder's review, Nov 20 | The builder agreed with the choices: a closed board refuses hiding, and a hidden wording keeps its place in its row. PR 76's changes from the same review are merged in: the reporter's "You reported this", and the note for Something else in an Editor's flag, which here is also the button to Hide. `card-menu.spec.ts:71`, which the builder approved changing for Report, also expects Hide in the owner's menu. Types, lint, the report and moderation API tests, and the report, moderation, and menu browser tests pass |

It changes the same choice as the first-visit notice and the privacy page (kept in this browser), so the notice goes and the privacy page agrees. It says "Usage analytics are off. Nothing more is sent from this browser." or "Usage analytics are on.", and the cursor stays on the button. With Global Privacy Control or Do Not Track: Off, "Your browser asks sites not to track you.", and no button. With no PostHog key (as locally), no row |
| Sign out | A button with the log-out icon above Delete account. It ends the session on the service (Better Auth's sign-out, already let through), forgets the tab's test account in development, and opens the home page, which shows Sign in. A reload stays signed out; the old session cookie, sent again from another browser, is refused (401), so the session is ended on the service and not only forgotten here; the next sign-in reaches the same account. If the service can't be reached: "Couldn’t sign you out. Try again." |
| The builder's review (Nov 21) | The descriptions under Privacy are now full sentences: "Steelman sends PostHog the pages you visit, what you click, and replays of your screen. Anything you type is hidden." and "Download a copy of everything you wrote, voted on, and assessed." (was "What you wrote, voted, and assessed"). The builder asked for it, so `e2e/account.spec.ts`'s check of the old Your data wording was changed to the new one, the one existing test this touches (AGENTS.md rule 9; named in the pull request) |
| Layout | A long hint squeezed the row's value to "O." and wrapped Turn off; on Your account a row's value and buttons now keep their width and the hint wraps instead. Checked at 1280 and 375 px |
| Second reader | A fresh agent found nothing blocking. Taken from its points: the sign-out test now replays the old cookie; a missing sign-out route counts as signed out only when acting as a test account; the row follows a choice made on the first-visit notice; the cursor returns to Sign out if it fails; the switch is named "Turn off usage analytics"; the hint says it goes to PostHog; Turn off is checked to reach PostHog as opt_out_capturing; M-07's audit row updated |

## November 21 — Server events (M-08)

Build plan M2-12, with Q29 (Nov 17): events from the service, anonymous like the browser's, only when the person's analytics choice allows. It changes what's collected, so the builder confirms the pull request.

| Check | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| What's sent | When a write is saved (not when it was already done or was refused): `vote_importance`, `vote_articulation`, `assess`, `write_argument`, `write_wording`, `edit_wording`, `add_source`. Each goes to PostHog's capture address with the project key, a new random ID each time, and the properties `environment` (local, staging, or production, from the address the service was reached at) and `source: service`, plus PostHog's flags for no person profile and no location lookup. No account, username, email, board, or text |
| When | Only when the write carries `x-steelman-analytics: on`, which the browser adds while its analytics are on (including while the first-visit notice is still asking, as the browser's own events do). Never with Global Privacy Control or Do Not Track (the service checks the headers too), never after Turn off, and never from a service with no PostHog key |
| Never in the way | The send isn't waited for, gives up after 2 seconds, and a failure is dropped: a PostHog that's down doesn't slow or fail the write (tested) |
| Update | Not sent: Update happens in the browser, from changes it already has, so the service never sees it. The browser's own analytics already record the click. Sending it would need a new request just for analytics; left for the builder |
| Privacy page | A paragraph under Cookies and analytics: "While your analytics are on, our service also tells PostHog each time you save a vote, an assessment, or something you wrote…", and it says what the events don't carry. Dated November 21 |
| Second reader | A fresh agent found nothing blocking, and confirmed nothing in an event or its request identifies a person (the service forwards no browser headers; PostHog sees the service's address, with location lookup off), the header is never on a read, queued writes check the choice when they're sent, and a failed send can't reject unhandled. Taken from its points: the service releases PostHog's answer; a PostHog that never answers is tested not to hold up the write (under 1 s); the browser tests wait for analytics to start before voting; a test that Turn on from the privacy page brings the header back; the privacy page says "each time you vote, assess, or save something you wrote" (a withdrawn vote is an event too) and that the random IDs aren't the browser's, so the events can't be joined to visits. Left for the builder: on Cloud Run the service has CPU only while answering a request, so an event sent just after a write may be slowed or lost when nothing else comes in; keeping CPU on costs money |
| Tests first | `server/test/api/server-events.test.ts` (6) and `e2e/server-events.spec.ts` (4, a fourth after the second reader), seen to fail first (two browser tests passed from the start, since nothing sent the header yet) |
| Known limitations | PostHog sees the service's address, not the person's, so the events have no location; with the browser's events off, nothing joins them to a visit. Not tested against PostHog itself (a stand-in receives them) |

## November 22 — "edited" stays open when clicked after hovering (A-06)

A bug fix in M2-5's "edited" (the wording it replaced and the votes before). Found as a flaky browser test: `e2e/edited-counts.spec.ts`'s "with a mouse, a click keeps it open…" failed about 2 runs in 10, on its own branch as well as others.

| Check | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| The bug | A click set the panel to stay open only if it wasn't already showing. With a real mouse, hovering shows it before the click, so the click closed it. The test passed only when its click came before the page caught up with the hover |
| The fix | A click goes by whether the panel is held open, not whether it's showing: the first click holds open what hovering showed (or opens it, on a phone), and a second click closes it |
| Tests first | A new test hovers, waits for the panel, then clicks and moves away; it failed 3 runs in 3 before the fix. After: the five tests in `e2e/edited-counts.spec.ts` passed 50 runs in 50. The existing test is unchanged. A sixth, after the second reader: Enter opens it and Enter again closes it |
| Second reader | A fresh agent found nothing blocking. Taken: the new test also checks aria-expanded after the second click; the keyboard test |

## November 22 — Changing your email, built (L-04)

Build plan M2-8c, built from the design approved on Nov 22 (PR 82, after Notion). Better Auth already has this flow (its email-code plugin's change email), so no package was added.

| Check | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| Compared first (AGENTS.md rule 12) | Notion, as for the design: a code to the new address, the old address told, no recent sign-in asked for, Google tied to the email |
| The steps | The Email row's Change opens New email with Cancel and Send code. Then "Confirm your new email": "We sent a code to x. Your email changes when you enter it.", the sign-in screen's six code boxes (now one shared piece), Send a new code, Cancel, and Change email. Enter submits; Escape cancels. On success: "Your email is now x. We sent a note to your old email." and the cursor goes back to Change. Cancel says "Your email hasn’t changed." |
| The code | Sign-in's rules and messages: six digits, ten minutes, five wrong tries, a new code cancels the old. Asking counts against the new address's five emails an hour, and against the network's 30 codes an hour that signing in uses (architecture §7's table says so) |
| An address another account uses | No code is sent and the form still says one was, so it can't be used to find out who has an account (Better Auth's own behavior). The address is emailed instead: "You already have a Steelman account with this email", so its owner isn't left waiting. If another account takes the address after the code was sent, the right code is refused with "Another account already uses this email." |
| The old address | Gets "Your Steelman email changed. The email for your Steelman account is now x. If you didn't make this change, reply to this email." If that email fails, the change still stands, and the log says so without an address |
| Google | A Google account joined through the old address is let go, so Google signs in only with the new address, as the Your account row says. It joins again if its email is the new one |
| Privacy page | "Resend sends the codes you use to sign in or to change your email. When your email changes, it also sends a note to your old address. If you ask to change to an email another account already uses, it tells that address instead of sending a code." How long codes are kept now covers both kinds. Dated November 22 |
| Second reader | A fresh agent found three things that blocked, all fixed with a test first: a request the service refused before checking the code (one with no code) still let go of Google and sent the old address a false note, so the note now goes only when the account's email really changed; codes for a new email weren't counted against the network's limit, so one account could email address after address; and stray blank lines. Also taken: deleting an account removes its waiting change-email code; the form's errors are announced; a cancelled Send code no longer reopens the code step; Escape has a test; the in-use check compares in lower case; a failure after the change is logged, not shown as an error; the wording "We’ve sent too many codes to this address." |
| In the running app | Checked by hand with `npm run dev`: signed in, changed the email, entered the code from the terminal; the note to the old address printed there |
| Tests first | `server/test/api/change-email.test.ts` (11), the network limit in `server/test/api/sign-in-network-limit.test.ts` (1), and the Resend tests in `server/test/deployed.test.ts` (4), then `e2e/change-email.spec.ts` (5, plus accessibility scans at 1280 and 375 px), all seen to fail first. `server/test/api/smoke.test.ts`'s stand-in for email now has the new emails too (the interface grew); it checks nothing about them |
| Known limitations | Replies to the note go to the sending address (EMAIL_FROM), which must receive mail for "reply to this email" to reach anyone; it's the only way back for someone whose session was taken over, since their Google link goes and a code to the old address now makes a new account. After a change, Google with the old address makes a new, separate account. Sign-in's own "Too many codes sent to this address." and "Too many wrong tries." are still fragments; changing them touches sign-in's tests, so that's for the builder |

## November 22 — Options: add, rename, delete (A-07)

| Check | Observed result |

Notion, read live: "•••" to the right of a group's heading. Both delete a column with items in it, which then fall into an ungrouped column; Steelman refuses instead, since an argument has no column to fall into (plans/reviews/m3-design-audit.md) |
| Who may | An Editor or the Owner: a new `structure` action in the policy table, as permissions.md's matrix has it ("Combine, separate, edit options": Editor and Owner; a Contributor suggests, which is A-11, later). A Contributor, Voter, or Viewer sees neither the ⋯ nor "Add an option", and the service refuses them. A closed board refuses everyone |
| Rename | ⋯ "Options for Yes", then Rename option: the name becomes a field in the header. Enter or leaving it keeps the new name; Escape keeps the old. An empty name keeps the old one: "An option needs a name, so the old name stays." Done: "You renamed the option." Enter and Escape put the cursor back on ⋯; leaving the field by clicking elsewhere leaves it there |
| Add | The dashed "Add an option" after the last column opens a new column whose name field says "Name this option". Enter adds it: "You added the option “Only in winter”.", and the cursor goes to its "Add an argument". Escape adds nothing; Enter on an empty name says "An option needs a name, so none was added." At four options the button goes, and it stays away while a fifth would be possible only because one is held for Undo. On a phone the new option is the side shown |
| Delete | Delete option, only for an empty option and never below two. The item says why it can't be used ("A question needs at least two options." or "Move or delete its arguments first.") or that it can ("It has no arguments."). The column goes at once: "You deleted the option. Undo is available for ten seconds.", with the cursor on Undo, in Board and in Strongest case. Undo brings it back and sends nothing ("The option is back."), with the cursor on its ⋯; after ten seconds it's sent, as deleting a wording is, and the cursor goes to "Add an option". It stays off the screen until the service answers, and comes back with the reason if refused. Closing the tab sends it at once; closing it offline asks first. One Undo is held at a time: deleting a wording sends a held option delete, and the other way round. On a phone, deleting the side shown shows the first side |
| Other readers | A new option or a new name shows on their next poll. A deleted option, always empty, leaves on their next poll too, without waiting for Update (shared/src/sync.ts), and the pill doesn't count it. An argument queued offline for an option deleted meanwhile is refused: "1 action couldn't be sent: that option was deleted." |
| Change log | option_added, option_renamed, option_deleted, each with who, for History (M3a-4) |
| Tests first | `server/test/api/options.test.ts` (12) and `e2e/options.spec.ts` (8, with accessibility scans at 1280 and 375 px, the menu open and a new option being named), all seen to fail first except the Contributor case, which passed while there was nothing to see |
| Second reader | A fresh agent found four blocking problems, all fixed: a deleted option came back as an empty column, for the deleter between the send and the answer and for readers who had it on screen; closing the tab dropped a held option delete; at 375 px, deleting the side shown left an empty board and a new option was hidden; the role matrix lacked the three actions. Also taken: the argument refusal above, the focus rules, one Undo at a time, the Undo bar in Strongest case, the menu closing on scroll, the "It has no arguments." hint, the empty-name note, tracking a new option by ID, and Add an option at four. Tests for these: two in `shared/test/sync.test.ts`, one in `server/test/api/stale-writes.test.ts`, 21 role-matrix rows, and 11 in `e2e/options.spec.ts`. The fixes were written before their browser tests; with the fixes taken out, all 11 fail |
| Known limitations | Options can't be reordered (the prototype has no way either). Deleting an option isn't yet restorable after its ten seconds; History's Restore (M3a-4) will do that. When two Editors rename one option at once, the later name wins and neither is told. An argument queued offline for an option deleted meanwhile is lost with its text, since there's no column for its draft to come back to; the note says so |

## November 22 — Can’t open it? (C-02)

Build plan M3a-3, as the prototype's source row has it. The builder asked for design questions to be settled by copying credible products (Nov 22).

| Check | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| Compared first (AGENTS.md rule 12) | Wikipedia's Help:Archiving a source, read live Nov 22 for the M3 audit (`plans/reviews/m3-design-audit.md`): a dead link stays in the citation, marked dead, with an archived copy from the Wayback Machine. Taken: "Try an archived copy", a plain link to `web.archive.org/web/<address>`, and the source staying readable. The prototype: "Can’t open it?" in an open source, open to every role (`report-source: view`), and "Undo report" for the reporter |
| Who may (the open question in the build plan) | Anyone who can read reports, as the prototype has it and as anyone may report a wording (M2-3). The reporter withdraws their own report, as the prototype's "Undo report" does; an Editor or the Owner clears anyone's, as the audit recommended. A new `unreport` action in the policy table. Wikipedia lets any editor remove a dead-link tag; Steelman keeps clearing someone else's report to Editors, since a cleared mark lets a wording be assessed as supported again |
| Report | "Can’t open it?" marks the source; its accessible name is its visible text, so voice control finds it (WCAG 2.5.3). Its summary says Unavailable, and the open source says "You reported on Nov 16, 2026 that it can’t be opened." or names the reporter, then "Try an archived copy". The cursor stays on the source. Announced: "You reported that this source can’t be opened. It shows as unavailable on every wording that cites it." While a wording cites no other source, Yes is disabled, and the service refuses it (PRD §4) |
| Undo and clear | The reporter's "Undo report" says "You withdrew your report."; an Editor's or the Owner's "Clear report" says "You cleared the report, so the source shows as available." A Contributor who didn't report it sees neither. When someone else got there first, it says "Someone already reported that this source can’t be opened." or "The report was already cleared." |
| Other readers | The mark shows on every wording that cites the source, on their next poll, with who reported it |
| Change log | source_unavailable and source_available, each with who, for History (M3a-4). A second report changes nothing and uses no change number |
| Your data | Download your data lists every time you reported a source, from the change log, with when and whether the report still stands. Deleting your account leaves a report credited to deleted_account, as what you wrote is. The privacy page now lists your reports under What we collect, and under Who sees what says that everyone on the board sees who reported a source, and that the history keeps it after it's cleared. The date always gives the year, so an old report can’t look recent |
| Tests first | `server/test/api/source-unavailable.test.ts` (9) and `e2e/source-unavailable.spec.ts` (6, with accessibility scans at 1280 and 375 px), each seen to fail first, except "a source that isn't on the board isn't found", which passed while there was no route. The two role-matrix rows were written after the service, so weren't seen to fail; for them, Cara's source in the shared fixture is now reported by Cara, which no other row depends on |
| Simulation | Reporting and clearing the board's source are now steps, with their own check 9 (anyone may report; only the reporter or the owner clears), and `server/test/simulation/source-reports.test.ts` always plays them. It found one thing: the simulation's model expected a repeated Yes to be refused once its source was reported, while the service answers "already done", since sending twice is once and an assessment made before a report stands. The model now agrees with the service; this is a change to an existing check, for the builder to see |
| Second reader | A fresh agent found nothing blocking. Taken: the button's accessible name, the export from the change log, the narrower rule for a mark with no reporter, the "already" announcements, the privacy wording, the year, the build plan row, and the simulation steps |
| Looked at | The open source at 1280 and 375 px: the note and the archived-copy link wrap within the card |
| Known limitations | Reporting isn't queued offline, like reporting a wording: offline, it says it couldn’t reach Steelman. A source with no web address gets no archived-copy link. On a closed board, "Can’t open it?" still shows, and the service refuses it, as Remove does; closing a board isn't in the app yet (M3b) |

## November 22 — History (J-03)

Build plan M3a-4. The plan asked for the design of what History leaves out, and whether it filters, to be shown first; the builder asked for design questions to be settled by copying credible products (Nov 22).

| Check | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| Compared first (AGENTS.md rule 12) | Google Docs' Version history, read live Nov 22 for the M3 audit: grouped by day, who and when, Restore this version. Google Drive's Activity panel, from memory: one sentence per change ("Kieran renamed an item"), grouped by day, with an avatar. Wikipedia's page history, from memory: public to every reader. The prototype: the clock in the question header opens its drawer, with an avatar, the person, what they did, and when |
| What it shows (the design question) | Everything in the change log, for everyone on the board: options added, renamed, deleted, and restored; wordings edited, deleted, hidden, and restored, with the moderator's reason; sources added, cited, taken off, edited, reported, and cleared; changes of access and roles; the board's creation. Left out, because the log doesn't hold them: votes, assessments, reports of wordings, and new arguments and wordings. No filter, as Google Docs' version history has none by default |
| The panel | "History", in words beside the clock, at the top right of the question card (the audit asked for words); on a phone it sits on its own line above the question, so a long question never runs under it. It opens the prototype's drawer, "History is open.", with the cursor on Close; Escape or Close shuts it, "History closed.", with the cursor back on History. On a wide screen the board moves left to make room, as the prototype's does; on a phone it covers the board |
| Entries | Grouped under the day, as "Nov 16, 2026", newest first; each is a sentence, "maya.chen deleted the option “Only in winter”.", with its time. A wording is quoted and links to itself on the board; a hidden wording is quoted only for an Editor or the Owner ("hid a wording as off-topic." for others); a deleted wording isn't quoted, since its words are erased |
| Restore | On the newest removal of each thing, while it's still gone and the reader may bring it back: a deleted option (an Editor or the Owner; never past four), a source taken off a wording (anyone who may add a source; the credibility score starts over, as with any change of sources), and a hidden wording (an Editor or the Owner). Each is a change of its own, so History shows it ("maya.chen restored the option “Only in winter”."). Its accessible name says what it restores ("Restore the option “Only in winter”"), as GOV.UK adds hidden context to a repeated link. Restoring a hidden wording says "You restored the wording."; putting a source back says "You put the source back on the wording. The credibility score resets." An option held for Undo still counts toward the four |
| Other readers | An open History reads the log again when a poll brings a new change-log row, so others' changes arrive on the next poll, and votes, which aren't in it, don't make it read again |
| Never anyone's words | The service sends IDs, kinds, times, names of who acted, and reasons, never text (invariant 7); the board names things from the rows the reader already has |
| Tests first | `server/test/api/history.test.ts` (8) and `e2e/history.spec.ts` (10, with accessibility scans at 1280 and 375 px). The first 8 browser tests were seen to fail first; the 2 added after the review (no Restore at four options; the sentences for access, roles, and source reports) passed at once. The server tests were seen to fail first, except "History holds no one's words", which passed while there was no route. The role matrix gained "read the board's history" (a Viewer may) and "restore a deleted option" (an Editor may), written after the service |
| Looked at | The panel at 1280 px, beside the board, and at 375 px, over it; a long question beside the History button at 1280 px and below it at 375 px |
| Second reader | A fresh agent found nothing blocking. Taken: History reads again only when the log moves, not on every vote; a person with no username shows as "someone"; Restore's accessible name; a rename says "renamed the option now called “C”.", which stays true after later renames; the History announcements are sentences; an unknown option isn't quoted; Restore waits while it's sent; a link opens in a new tab with a modifier key held; the panel's title has its own ID; the layout with a long question; the two tests above |
| Known limitations | A deleted wording can't be restored after its ten seconds: deleting erases its words (A-06), so the ten-second Undo stays its only way back, which WCAG 2.2.1 counts as a time limit. Keeping deleted words to allow it would change what Steelman keeps about people, so it's the builder's decision. History reads at most the newest 500 changes. A role change names the person by their username now, not then. On a phone the panel covers the board but isn't modal, so Tab can move into the board behind it, as the prototype's drawer allows. The build plan asked for the design to be shown in the prototype first; it was settled by comparison with credible products instead, as the builder asked on Nov 22 |

| What Combine moves (the builder's choice, Nov 22) | The wording you drag or choose, as the prototype always did, with its articulation votes, assessments, and comments. The two arguments become one only when it was its argument's last wording; then importance votes merge per person. A first version moved whole arguments; the builder preferred the prototype's model |
| The dialogs (the builder: "far too wordy") | Only the question, the wordings, and the buttons. A wording from an argument with others: "Combine this wording with that argument?", the wording, "joins", the other argument's lead, then Cancel and Combine. Its argument's last wording: "Combine these arguments?", the same pair, and one line only when someone must vote again ("You’ll be asked to vote again." or "2 people will be asked to vote again."). Separate: "Make this wording its own argument?", the wording, then Cancel and Separate |
| Ways in | "Combine here" from the ⋯ menu, dragging the merge handle onto another argument, Combine on a suggestion, "Make it a separate argument", and the column's separate slot: each opens the dialog first. Cancel, Escape, or a click outside changes nothing |
| After | The ten-second Undo still follows, and History lists the change. When your own two votes disagreed, the combined argument says "Your votes on the two arguments disagreed, so vote again." until you vote |
| Checked in a browser | Headless Chromium on the first version: both paths reach the dialog, at 1280 and 375 px. On the revised version: dragging one wording out of an argument with three opens "Combine this wording with that argument?" and moves only it. Re-checked after compacting, at 1280 and 375 px, by the menu path: a one-wording argument opens "Combine these arguments?" with "You and 2 others will be asked to vote again.", Combine says "The arguments are combined." and shows the vote-again note; Separate opens "Make this wording its own argument?"; Escape closes it; nothing runs past the screen's edge. The dialog sits beside the argument it's about, so on a phone it can open partly below the fold until focus moves to its button |
| Known limitations | Suggesting a combination (A-11) adds a suggestion without a dialog; accepting one opens it. The prototype's History lists a combination but can't undo it yet: that's M3a-8's design |

## November 22 — Combine and separate on the service (A-10, J-05)

Build plan M3a-6: the service only; the board comes in M3a-7. Built to the model the builder chose for M3a-5: Combine moves one wording.

| Check | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| Combine | `POST /api/boards/:board/versions/:id/combine {into}`, an Editor's or the Owner's (the structure action). The wording moves into the other argument in its option; its ID never changes, so its articulation votes, assessments, and link go with it (Q8). Another option, a deleted wording, or an argument that's gone is refused; into the argument it's already in is already done |
| The last wording | When it leaves its argument empty, the two arguments become one: the empty one keeps its row with `merged_into`, and importance votes merge per person (PRD §5). Two identical votes become one; two that disagree are both removed and the person is asked to vote again (`askedAgain` on their row, until they vote); a vote on only one moves. A hidden wording counts as one left behind |
| Separate | `POST /versions/:id/separate {argumentId}`: the wording becomes its own argument, at the end of its option, with no importance votes. An argument's only wording isn't separated |
| Undo | `PUT /changes/:seq/undo`, as a change of its own: the wording goes back, and if the arguments became one, the votes come back and `merged_into` clears. A wording added since stays where it was added, and a vote cast since on the combined argument wins over the one it would restore. Undoing twice is already done; a wording moved again since is refused (`combined_since`); anything but a combine or a separation is refused |
| Never anyone's votes in the log | The change log holds the wording, the two arguments, and whether they became one (invariant 7). Each person's votes before the merge are kept in a new table, `combine_vote`, so Undo can bring them back; deleting an account or a board deletes its rows |
| Stale writes (architecture §3) | An importance vote or a new wording sent to an argument combined away goes to the argument it went into (your vote there stands); an articulation vote on a wording that moved applies |
| Other readers | A moved wording stays where the reader sees it, marked, until Update; a new argument made only of wordings shown elsewhere waits for Update too |
| Tests first | `server/test/api/combine.test.ts` (28), written for whole-argument combine first and seen to fail, then rewritten for one wording; the role matrix gained combine, separate, and undo (an Editor may); `shared/test/sync.test.ts` gained the moved-wording rules; the simulation gained combine, separate, and undo steps, with one fixed sequence that always plays them (`server/test/simulation/combine.test.ts`), and its ten checks hold |
| Second reader | A fresh agent found two blocking problems, both fixed with a test seen to fail first: deleting an account missed its set-aside votes on a board where those were its only mark, so an Undo could bring them back; and undoing two combines into one argument out of order put a vote on an argument its person never voted on, so a combine is now undone only after any later one that combined the same argument. Also taken: a vote cast after a combine, even a withdrawal, is marked as newer, so Undo leaves it as it is; someone asked to vote again stays asked when that argument is combined into another; Download your data includes set-aside votes, and the privacy page says how long they're kept; the refusals say what changed (`version_deleted`, `argument_deleted`, `combined_since`); the simulation undoes out of order; and eight more API tests |
| Known limitations | `combine_vote` is a new table, an addition to architecture §2 that waits for the builder's approval, and it keeps set-aside votes for as long as the board exists, so any combine can be undone (rule 10: what's kept about people). The schema-shape test's list of board tables gained it, as M2-3's did. Sending a combine again after it was undone combines again, and sending it again after the wording moved on moves it back: rare, but short of invariant 10. A hidden wording can be combined or separated, and one left behind keeps its argument. Undoing a separation leaves any votes on the new argument on it, closed. History names these changes in M3a-8, which also undoes them after the ten seconds |

## November 22 — M3a audit: code and concision (A-07, A-10, C-02, J-03, J-04, J-05)

An audit of pull requests 93 to 99 (M3a-2 to M3a-8) at the builder's request: whether the code works and follows good practice, and whether the screens say only what they need to, with icons and layout doing the rest. Three readers: one of the service, one of the browser code, and a walk through every M3a screen in the browser at 1360, 800, and 375 px on a scratch board.

| Check | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| The check before any change | `npm run check` on `combine-history`, Node 22, Docker: 1,014 unit, service, and simulation tests pass; 329 browser tests pass and 10 fail. The 10 are the ones already waiting for the builder: 8 saved screenshots (M3a-3's "Can’t open it?" and M3a-4's History button) and the 2 menu tests in `e2e/card-menu.spec.ts` that pin the menu without combining. CI on 94 to 98 fails for those 10 alone |
| Compared first (AGENTS.md rule 12) | From memory, not read live: Notion's and Trello's column menus say "Rename" and "Delete" without repeating "column" or "list"; menu commands that ask something more before acting end in "…" (Apple's and Microsoft's interface guidelines, GitHub's "Transfer issue…"); GitHub puts Revert on a merge's own line beside its time; Gmail's Undo bar is one short sentence and Undo. Taken: each of those |
| Fault: an argument left empty by Undo (A-10, J-04) | Undoing a combine that made two arguments one, after the second's own wording was combined elsewhere, deleted by its author, or (through a separation) moved back, left the second argument on the board with no wording, still taking votes and keeping its option from being deleted. It closes now, as a separation's new argument does. `server/test/api/combine-undo-emptied.test.ts`: 3 of 4 failed first. The simulation now checks after every step that every argument on the board has a wording |
| Fault: two options with one name (A-07) | New steelman refuses options with the same name; adding, renaming, and restoring one didn't check. Refused now as `option_name_taken`, capitals aside ("Another option already has that name."); a deleted option's name is free again. `server/test/api/option-names.test.ts`: 3 failed first |
| Fault: Undo counted as writing (J-03, J-04) | Undoing a combine and restoring an option counted toward the 60-an-hour writing limit, which architecture §7 says undoing never does, so an Editor at the limit had no way back. `server/test/api/undo-not-limited.test.ts` failed first |
| Fault: "Add an option" under the board at tablet widths (A-07) | From 641 to 800 px the stylesheet fixed the board at two columns, so "+ Option" (and a third option) wrapped to a second row, into the 40 px gap. That width now uses the columns the board sets, in the prototype's stylesheet, which the app shares |
| Less text (the builder: remove text, use icons and layout) | Option menu: "Rename" and "Delete"; the note under an enabled Delete ("It has no arguments.") is gone; a disabled one says "Only an empty option can be deleted." ("Move or delete its arguments first." named a move that doesn't exist, and a delete only each author can do). Wording menu: "Combine…" and "Separate…", each one line. The combine handle's hint: "Combine". The dialog: an arrow between the two wordings in place of the word "joins" (still read to a screen reader). Vote again: a chip under the card, "Vote again", with the vote it asks for ringed; why ("Your votes on the two arguments disagreed.") is its hint and what a screen reader hears. Source report: "You reported it on Nov 16, 2026." beside the Unavailable tag |
| Cleaner layout | "Combine here" sits over the card's foot, so the wording being chosen stays readable (it was veiled, behind the button). The vote-again note no longer runs under the stack's edge, nor stretches it. History: a wording's link is ink, underlined, not the browser's blue; an entry's time and its Restore or Undo share one line; a sentence ending in a quoted wording that ends in a full stop no longer reads “…to study.”. On a phone the "Pick the argument…" banner clears the "Most important first" label |
| The prototype | The same stylesheet rules, and the same labels for the same things (option menu, Combine…, Separate…, the handle's hint, Vote again, the dialog's arrow), then `scripts/sync-prototype.py`. Opened at 768 px: no console errors, the option menu reads Rename and Delete, and "+ Option" sits beside the columns |
| Browser faults fixed (the second and third readers) | An edit in progress is no longer closed, with what was typed, when a poll brings someone else's combine of that wording. Undo of your own combine after you pressed Update no longer redraws the board as it was before Update (it moved rows back and brought deleted rows back). A read that fails just after a combine, an undo, or an option delete is no longer an unhandled error that lost the Undo bar or left the option counted for good. A History or Strongest case link to a wording someone moved, before Update, now says "Press Update to see that wording where it is now." in the header's note and leaves the board alone (it folded the open row and opened nothing). The dialog and "Combine here" never name a deleted or moved-out wording. A wording someone moved reads "This wording moved to “…”", whatever moved it (it said "combined with" after an undo too). Picking ends when the reader switches to Strongest case, chooses Separate…, or loses the role, and an Escape that closes a menu, History, or a field no longer also cancels it. A second press on "Can’t open it?" while the first is on its way does nothing. History's Restore no longer counts an option held for Undo twice. The archived-copy link is built from the address in its standard form. A row's hooks run in one order (a row whose only wording was hidden, then restored, could have stopped the board). `e2e/m3a-audit.spec.ts` (4, each failing on the code before); `shared/test/sync-undo-after-update.test.ts` (3) |
| Checked | `npm run check`, Node 22, Docker: 1,025 unit, service, and simulation tests pass (11 new); 333 browser tests pass (4 new) and the same 10 fail, still waiting for the builder. Types, lint, and `git diff --check` are clean |
| Existing tests changed (rule 9) | Five browser specs from this same stack of pull requests pin wording this audit changed at the builder's request, and now pin the new wording: `options.spec.ts` (menu labels and notes), `combine.spec.ts` (menu labels, the vote-again chip, the moved-wording sentence), `history.spec.ts` and `history-undo.spec.ts` (a sentence's final full stop), `source-unavailable.spec.ts` (the report line). No assertion was removed. They aren't on `main` yet, but the protected-files check lists them against this pull request's base, so it waits for `builder-approved`. The 2 `card-menu.spec.ts` tests and 8 screenshots that already waited still do: the menu they'll pin now reads Copy link, Edit wording and context, Combine…, Separate…, Delete version |
| Left for the builder (rule 10, or a design call) | Editing a reported source's link doesn't clear its Unavailable mark, and the person who added it can't clear someone else's report (Wikipedia's editors remove a dead-link tag when they fix the link; doing the same lets a Contributor do more than before). A queued change to an importance vote on an argument combined away is dropped when the combine carried that person's vote over (architecture §3 read literally). History lists no new arguments or wordings (the log doesn't hold them), which is most of what happens on a board. On a phone, History takes a line of its own above the question. What the service refuses or can't be reached for ("Another option already has that name.", "…this can’t be undone yet.") is said only in the status line, which a screen reader hears and no one sees; that was so before M3a, and the prototype has no place for it either |
| Known limitations, found and not fixed | History names a hidden wording's sources to everyone, since every reader is sent every source (before M3a). A retried add-option after a rename, or separate after a later move, answers `id_taken`, not `already_done`. After undos out of order, a combined-away argument can point at an argument its wording has since left. `combine_vote` has no change-number trigger, and voting updates it without an index that fits. Dragging is tested in Chromium alone, and a drag whose handle leaves the page mid-drag can leave the drop targets marked. When someone else combines a row a reader is drafting a wording in, the draft still becomes a new argument on Update, and an open source form closes. After a held option delete, coming back through the browser's back-forward cache can leave it counted toward the four until reload |

## November 22 — M3a audit, the builder's decisions (M-14, M-07, C-02, A-10, J-03)

The builder decided the five items the M3a audit left open, all as recommended. Each with tests written first and seen to fail.

|---|---|
| Write-up | `SECURITY.md` at the root holds the checks, the findings, the fixes, the remaining issues, and the attack log |

| Local, two accounts | Account B was refused with 403 on six requests against Account A's invited-only board. A script in a wording and a `javascript:` source link were shown as plain text |

| Production backup | An on-demand Cloud SQL backup was taken with the builder's approval (ID `1795407059434`) |
| Fixes | Pull requests 102 (L-01), 103 (L-01, L-04), and 104 (M-12), each with new tests that failed first. Types, lint, and the unit and service tests passed locally for each; the browser tests ran in CI |
| Product Page | PRD v3 has a "Hosting, database, and security" table, and the build log has the Build 3 entry. Both were checked at desktop width and at 375 px with no sideways scroll |
| Known limitations | The two-account test on production waits for the builder, who can sign in as two real accounts. The live app has no PostHog key yet. The low findings are in issue 105 |

Build plan M3a-9: someone signed in who opens the link of a board they aren't invited to sees a card in place of two plain lines.

| Item | Result |
|---|---|
| The card | The card is the one "This steelman doesn't exist" uses. Its title is "You need access". It says "You don't have access to this steelman. The owner hasn't shared it with this account." and "You're signed in as" with the person's email. Its button is Go to Your steelmen and its link is Sign in with another account. When the card appears, its title takes the keyboard's place, so a screen reader reads it, and no ring is drawn around the title |
| Checked by machine | `app/e2e/no-access.spec.ts` has ten browser tests. A visitor signs in by email code, opens an invited-only board's link, and sees the card with their email and both ways on, at 1280 and 375 px, with no sideways scroll and a clean axe scan. Go to Your steelmen opens the list. Sign in with another account ends the session, shows Sign in at the same link, and shows the board at that link once a member signs in. The same person signing in again sees the card again. When the service can't sign them out, the card says "Steelman couldn't sign you out. Try again." and they stay signed in. Someone reading a board with the keyboard on a wording is shown the card when the owner turns the link off, and the keyboard's place moves to the card's title. A link to a board that doesn't exist still shows "This steelman doesn't exist". A tab acting as a test account shows that account's username, because test accounts have no email |
| Tests first | Eight of the tests were written before the card and failed because the old screen had no "You need access" title. The missing-board test passed before and after, as it should. The test of a test account signing out was added after the card was built. After the second-agent review, three checks were added to these tests and failed before the fix: the title has the keyboard's place when the owner turns the link off, no ring is drawn around the title, and the sign-out error names Steelman |
| The whole check | `npm run check` passed locally on Node 22 with Docker: 1,091 unit, service, and simulation tests and 390 browser tests. `git diff --check` reported nothing. The two older tests that expect "You don't have access to this steelman." and Go to Your steelmen pass unchanged. After the review fixes, `npm run check` ran twice, and types, lint, and the 1,091 tests passed both times. Each run failed one browser test that this change doesn't touch while the machine was busy: the Share panel's Tab order in `share-open.spec.ts` the first time, and a rate-limited vote in `rate-limits.spec.ts` the second. Each of those files passed when run alone, and a third run of the browser tests passed all 390 |

## November 24 — Closing on the service (I-05, J-03)

Build plan M3b-3: the Owner closes a board now, sets or clears a moment it closes, and reopens it, on the service. The screens come in M3b-5 and M3b-6; this item's only screen work is History's four sentences.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| The writes | `PUT /api/boards/:board/closed` closes the board now, `DELETE` on the same address reopens it, and `PUT /api/boards/:board/closing` sets or clears the moment it closes. All three are the Owner's (the `close` rule, allowed on a closed board), go through the write path, and write a change row: `board_closed`, `board_reopened`, `closing_date_set`, or `closing_date_cleared`. A closing time is kept in its row as a number, because the change log holds no text. A moment that isn't ahead of now is refused as invalid, so Close now is the way to close at once. Reopening clears a moment that has passed and keeps one still ahead, as the prototype does. |
| The rule | A migration adds `board.closes_at`; `closed_at` keeps meaning closed by hand. One shared rule, `closedSince`, says a board is closed when it was closed by hand or its moment has passed. The write path asks it with the service's clock, so `writeToBoard` is handed the clock at all 27 of its call sites, with no change in what they do. The combine preview, which assumed an open board, asks it too. Nothing is written or scheduled when the moment passes. |
| What readers get | The snapshot's `board.closed` follows the rule. A `closing` object (`closed`, `closedAt`, `closesAt`) rides beside `access` and `role` on the snapshot, on every changes answer, and on the list, only when a board is closed or has a closing time, so every other answer keeps its shape. |
| History | History reads the four rows as "closed the board.", "reopened the board.", "set the board to close on Nov 30 at 5 PM EST.", and "removed the closing date.", with the moment in the reader's own time zone, named. |
| Checked by machine | `app/shared/test/closing.test.ts` has four unit tests of the rule: closed by hand; a millisecond before the moment is open; at the moment and after is closed; both dates set. `app/server/test/api/closing.test.ts` has eighteen service tests, which move the fake clock forward and never wait: the three writes across the seven personas, with a refusal using no change number; the Owner alone may reopen and set or clear a moment on a closed board; closed by the route, thirteen kinds of write are refused `board_closed` and every role still reads; reopening lets writes through; with a moment an hour ahead a vote is taken, and after the clock passes it, with no write between, the same vote is refused, the change number hasn't moved, the next changes answer says closed, and the snapshot's `board.closed` is true; six queued kinds are refused after a close by time; reopening clears a past moment and keeps one ahead; a past or present moment, a sentence, and a missing field are refused; the snapshot, both changes answers, and the list carry the closing object; an open board with no moment answers and lists exactly as before; each write sent twice changes nothing the second time; the four rows hold no text and read `deleted_account` after the owner's account is deleted; and History gives a set moment with its entry. `app/e2e/closing-history.spec.ts` checks History's four sentences in New York's time zone. The existing migration test applies the new migration as an upgrade of a database that holds rows. |
| Tests first | All the tests were written first. Eighteen of the nineteen unit and service tests failed, and the one that passed, that an open board answers exactly as before, should pass before and after. The History test failed without History's four sentences and passed with them. Two of the service tests were wrong as first written and were fixed before the change was finished: they moved the fake clock backwards, which it doesn't allow, and one read the change number before a read that publishes the shared order. |
| Products compared (rule 12) | Google Classroom's due date, read live on November 24: it takes a date and, optionally, a time of day, so a closing time here is one moment with both. Whether Classroom or Google Forms refuses a time already past isn't on Classroom's help page, so refusing a past moment follows them from memory only. |
| Second reader | Nothing high or medium was found. The fixes for its low findings: a snapshot's `board.closed` and its closing object are worked out at one moment, so they can't disagree; a change row without a usable moment no longer breaks History; History's year is compared with the entry's own time, not the reader's clock. Tests were added for the time format (`app/web/test/moment-with-zone.test.ts`: minutes, summer time, another year), the combine preview on a board closed by its time, and two behaviours now pinned: a later time on a board its time has closed opens it again until then, as the prototype does, and Close now on such a board is already done. These were added after the change, so they weren't seen failing first. |
| Left out | The Decision block (I-06, T5). The screens for a closed board and the Owner's controls come in M3b-5 and M3b-6. |

## November 24 — Closing in the simulation (I-05)

Build plan M3b-4: the simulation closes boards by hand and by their time, and checks that a closed board refuses every write.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| The steps | `world.ts`, a helper and not a test file, gains two of the Owner's steps with low weights: close now, and a closing time one or two minutes ahead, which the existing minute step carries the board over. Each is paired with a reopen a few steps later, which also clears the closing time, so the other checks still see enough saved actions. Only one closing is under way at a time. |
| The checks | `expectedRefusal` answers `board_closed` for each of the seven kinds of action while the board is closed by hand or its time has passed, before whose item it is and what went stale, as the write path orders them; everyone in the simulation may write, so a role too low doesn't arise. Hiding, restoring, combining, separating, undoing, and reporting or clearing a source each gain the closed case. A new check 11 says a closed board refuses every write, closed by hand or by its time. The snapshot reads are given the fake clock. The tally counts boards closed by hand and by time, each kind refused while closed, and each done again after a reopen. |
| Checked by machine | `app/server/test/simulation/closing.test.ts` has two fixed sequences, each on its own board: closed by hand, and closed by its time. In each, the tally shows the board closed that way, each of the seven kinds refused once while closed and done again after the reopen, and closing sent twice changes nothing the second time. `app/server/test/simulation/planted-closing.test.ts` plants a write path that ignores `closes_at`, and the simulation catches it with check 11 and shrinks the sequence. `simulation.test.ts`, `stale-refusals.test.ts`, and `planted-bugs.test.ts` pass untouched. |

Low findings fixed: a close step now runs the always-true checks too, the closed case sits in `expectedRefusal` as the row says, and the header counts eleven checks. |

## November 24 — Comments on the service (D-01, M-07)

Build plan M3b-8: the service keeps comments, and the account pages say so. There is no screen for comments yet.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| The table | Migration `0014_comments.sql` adds `comment` as architecture §2 models it: a browser-made ID, the board, a target (an option, a version, or neither for the question, never both), a parent, the author, the body, when it was made, resolved, hidden, and deleted, and `changed_seq` with the stamp trigger. Its foreign keys include `board_id`, so a comment can point only at rows of its own board. The service may add and change a comment, never remove its row. There are no length or value checks in the database (Q24). |
| The policy | Two rows join the policy table, as permissions.md has them and as the builder approved on Nov 24: `comment`, for anyone who can read, and `uncomment`, for the comment's author only. Both are allowed on a closed board, so a person can always take their own words back. |
| The service | `POST /api/boards/:board/comments` adds a comment of up to 1,000 characters (`COMMENT_MAX`, kept apart from the prototype's `LIMITS`, which an existing test pins); line breaks are kept and only the ends are trimmed. A comment on a deleted or hidden wording, or on a deleted option, is refused. `DELETE /api/boards/:board/comments/:id` deletes your own, which erases its text and keeps its row. Both are safe to send twice. Neither adds a row to the change log, as the prototype logs none. Adding counts toward the writing limit until M3b-9 gives comments a limit of their own. |
| The account | Deleting an account erases the text and author of the person's comments and keeps each row as deleted; a board deleted with its owner keeps no comment text. Download your data lists the person's comments under `comments`. The privacy page names comments among what is kept and what deletion removes, and is dated November 24. Its last sentence now says the date at the top shows when what we keep last changed, which was the builder's choice on Nov 24 instead of a notice. The delete form adds "Your comments are deleted." and the deleted-account page adds "Your comments are gone too." SECURITY.md names comments among what is stored. |
| Checked by machine | `app/shared/test/policy-comments.test.ts` checks the two policy rows for every role on an open and a closed board. `app/server/test/database/comments.test.ts` has six tests: each target is accepted; another board's version, option, or parent is refused; two targets are refused; an unstamped write is refused; the service's role can't delete a row; and the table is indexed by board and change number. `app/server/test/api/comments.test.ts` has twelve: the matrix for adding and deleting across the seven personas; targets, text, and line breaks kept; the 1,000-character limit, an empty comment, and two targets; another board's target isn't found; a deleted wording, a hidden wording, and a deleted option are refused; sent twice, and the same ID with other text or from someone else; delete erases the text and keeps the row, and the add sent again afterwards doesn't bring it back; a comment that isn't there; no change rows; a closed board accepts both and still refuses a vote; and a comment follows its wording through combine and separate. `app/server/test/api/comments-deletion.test.ts` has four: deletion erases text and author; the ID scan stays clean with comments on the board; a deleted board keeps no comment text; and the export lists the person's comments and nobody else's. `app/e2e/comments-account.spec.ts` has three browser tests for the privacy page, the delete form, and the deleted-account page. `schema-shape.test.ts`'s list of tables gains `comment`, as the builder approved on Nov 24. |
| Tests first | All the tests were written first. 21 of the 24 tests in the four service files failed; the three that passed check what must not happen, such as a comment that isn't there being found. The three browser tests failed against the pages as they were. |
