## November 24 — Replies, resolving, and the comment limit on the service (D-01, M-12)

Build plan M3b-9: the service takes replies, resolves and reopens threads, and gives comments a limit of their own. There is no screen for comments yet.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| Replies | `POST /api/boards/:board/comments` with a `parentId` adds a reply under that thread's first comment. A reply names the same target as its thread and keeps it on its row. Anyone who can comment may reply to any thread, on a closed board too, under the `comment` action already in the policy table. A reply to a reply is refused as `not_a_thread`, a reply naming another target is refused as `not_same_thread`, and a reply to a resolved thread is refused as `thread_resolved` (architecture §3's row for a thread resolved meanwhile). A reply to a thread on a deleted or hidden wording is refused as a comment on it is. A reply to a first comment that its author deleted or a moderator hid is refused as `thread_deleted`, because the prototype draws no Reply on such a comment (`commentItem` in `steelman-refined.html`). The thread still reads. A parent on another board, or one that doesn't exist, isn't found. |
| Resolve and reopen | `PUT /api/boards/:board/comments/:id/resolved` resolves a thread and `DELETE` on the same path reopens it, as `/closed` does for a board. Both set or clear `resolved_at` on the first comment only, and the replies aren't touched. Anyone who can comment may do either to any thread, as in the prototype and Google Docs. Resolving or reopening a reply is refused as `not_a_thread`. Resolving or reopening a thread whose first comment was deleted or hidden is refused as `thread_deleted`, because the prototype draws neither button on such a comment. |
| Sent twice | A reply sent again is already done, even after its thread was resolved or its first comment was deleted, because the check for a resend comes before every refusal. The same ID with other text, under another thread, or from another person is refused as `id_taken`. Resolve sent again is already done and keeps the time it was first resolved, and reopen sent again is already done. Both stay already done after the first comment is deleted. None of these uses a change number. |
| The limit | Comments and replies count against a new `comment` limit of 60 an hour per account (architecture §7). Resolving, reopening, and deleting aren't counted. Comments no longer count toward the writing limit, which M3b-8 used until now. |
| Choices left open by the plan | Resolving a thread whose wording was deleted is allowed, since resolving only tidies what is there. The builder can change this. A thread whose first comment was deleted or hidden follows the prototype and takes no reply, resolve, or reopen. |
| The change log | Replying, resolving, and reopening add no row to it, as comments add none. |
| Checked by machine | `app/server/test/api/comment-replies.test.ts` has 21 tests: the matrix for replying and for resolving and reopening across the seven personas; a closed board accepts all three from a Viewer; a reply keeps its thread's target on the question, an option, and a wording; a reply to a reply, naming another target, to a thread resolved meanwhile, and to a thread on a deleted wording are refused; a reply to a deleted or hidden first comment is refused; a reply resent after its first comment was deleted is already done; another board's parent isn't found; resolve and reopen set and clear the flag; resolving or reopening a reply is refused; resolving or reopening a thread whose first comment was deleted or hidden is refused; another board's comment isn't found, with the service's own answer and not a missing route's; a reply sent twice, and the same ID with other text, thread, or author; a reply resent after its thread was resolved; resolve and reopen sent twice; deleting a first comment keeps its replies' rows and text; only a reply's author deletes it; and no change rows after three writes that succeed. `app/server/test/api/comment-limit.test.ts` has three: comments and replies share one limit, the next is refused with how long to wait and saves nothing, another person isn't affected, and an hour later it's allowed; resolving, reopening, and deleting go through past the limit; and comments don't use up the writing limit. The M3b-8 comment tests, the rate-limit tests, `undo-not-limited.test.ts`, `policy-comments.test.ts`, and `inputs.test.ts` still pass unchanged. |
| Tests first | Both files were written first. 20 of the 22 tests failed. The two that passed check what must not happen: resolving or reopening a comment that isn't there isn't found (the routes didn't exist yet), and the three writes add no change rows (nothing was written). Review then tightened both: each now checks the service's answer, so neither can pass on a missing route or a failed write. Review also added the deleted and hidden first-comment tests. With those refusals taken out of `comments.ts`, the two refusal tests failed, and with them back, all 24 pass. |
| Not checked | No person has replied to or resolved a thread yet; there is no screen for one until M3b-11. The browser's sentence for a refused reply comes in M3b-12. |

## November 24 — Comments reach every reader (D-01, E-03, M-02)

Build plan M3b-10: comments reach every reader in the snapshot and in "changes after *S*", and the list's last change counts them. There is no screen for comments yet, so nothing new is drawn.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| The reads | `readSnapshot` and `readChanges` (`read.ts`) send a `CommentRow` for each comment: its target, its parent for a reply, its author's ID and username, its text, when it was added, whether it is resolved (only a thread's first comment carries that flag), whether it's deleted or hidden, and its change number. The `comments` key is left out when there are none, as the question and the closing state are, so a board without comments answers exactly as before. A deleted comment arrives with no text. Nothing hides a comment until M3b-14, which decides who reads a hidden one; until then a hidden comment's text reaches nobody. |
| Comments while their subject is gone | Both reads leave out comments on a deleted or hidden wording and on a deleted option, for the Owner and Editors too. A comment resolved while its wording is hidden isn't sent either. When History's Restore brings a wording or an option back after *S*, the next answer sends that subject's comments again, as they are now, though they're older than *S*. The service keys this on the `version_restored` and `option_restored` rows of the change log, not on the wording's own change number, which every vote moves. A wording restored and hidden again after *S* keeps its comments out. A deleted wording never comes back, so its comments stay out. |
| Sync and the helpers | `BoardRows.comments` holds every comment sent, and `merge` (`sync.ts`) gains one line, so an answer received twice or out of order changes nothing, and comments never move what's on screen. `shared/src/comments.ts` gives `threadsOn` (a target's threads, oldest first, each with its replies), `openCount` (the number for a speech bubble), and `subjectStands`. The count is the prototype's (`openCount` in `steelman-refined.html`): replies count; deleted comments and resolved threads don't; hidden ones do. The helpers leave out a comment whose wording or option has gone, or that the reader doesn't hold, for a reader who already holds it. `threadsOn` leaves out a deleted reply and a deleted first comment with no reply left. The prototype does the same, except in one sequence: when a first comment is deleted while it has replies and those replies are deleted afterwards, the prototype keeps the empty "This comment was deleted." thread, and `threadsOn` leaves it out. This is a deliberate departure for now, and M3b-12 decides whether to match it. |
| The list | Adding a comment or a reply, resolving a thread, and deleting a comment each count as the board's last change (`list.ts`). Reopening a thread clears its resolved time, so it leaves no time behind, just as a withdrawn vote leaves none. A comment counts even when its wording has gone since. |
| The simulation | `world.ts` gains four steps: add a comment on the question, an option, or a wording; reply; resolve or reopen; and delete your own or try to delete someone else's. Each is checked against what the database says should happen, in the order `comments.ts` decides, and sent a second time to see it changes nothing (checks 9 and 5). A closed board refuses none of them. Check 4 now covers comment rows: one at or below the reader's change number is allowed only when that answer's log has a Restore of its wording or option. A new check says no answer or snapshot carries a comment whose wording or option is gone. Check 8 compares the comments a caught-up reader holds through `subjectStands`, the rule the speech bubbles and panel will use (M3b-11 to M3b-14), because the service never sends the removal of a comment whose wording was hidden after the reader got it. The new reader's side and every other row are compared exactly as before. Comment refusals go in a tally field of their own, so the fixed sequences' counts stay as they were. |
| Checked by machine | `app/server/test/api/comments-reading.test.ts` has 13 tests: a comment is in another reader's next answer once, with its author's username and time; a reply carries its parent and its thread's target; a resolve, a reopen, and a delete arrive as the same row changed, the delete with no text; the answer with nothing new is exactly what it was on a board with comments; an answer with other changes leaves the key out; the snapshot holds every comment oldest first, deleted ones with no text; a board with no comments has no key; a deleted wording, a hidden wording, and a deleted option take their comments out of the snapshot for a Contributor and the Owner; a changes answer leaves out comments changed on those; a reader who arrived while a wording was hidden, or while an option was deleted, gets its comments on the poll after Restore, once; a wording restored and hidden again keeps them out; and Restore resends only that subject's comments. `app/server/test/api/list-comments.test.ts` has one: a comment, a reply, a resolve, and a delete each make the board the most recent. `app/shared/test/comments.test.ts` has 13 tests of the count, the threads, and whether a subject stands, with gone and missing subjects. `app/shared/test/sync-comments.test.ts` has six: the snapshot's comments are held, new and changed rows merge by ID, an answer with none keeps what's held, one received twice or out of order changes nothing, and nothing on screen moves. `app/server/test/simulation/comments.test.ts` plays one fixed sequence with every step kind, a hide and a Restore, and a closed board, with every check after each step. A minute passes after each comment and reply, so each step lands on the thread the sequence names, and the test asserts exact counts: one reopen, six comments sent again after the Restore (the wording's comment and its reply, to each of the three readers), and three comment steps on the closed board. With the reopen step taken out, the test fails. `app/web/test/session-comments.test.ts` has one: when a poll makes the reader an Editor and a comment is added before the board is read again, the comment is kept from that re-read. `app/server/test/simulation/planted-comments.test.ts` has four: the same short sequence passes with the real code, and three bugs planted in `read.ts` are each caught: no resend after Restore (check 8), every comment in every answer (check 4), and comments on a hidden wording (the new check). `reading.test.ts`, `closing.test.ts`, `question-rename.test.ts`, `list.test.ts`, `sync.test.ts`, the M3b-8 and M3b-9 comment tests, the simulation's fixed sequences, 300 random sequences in `simulation.test.ts`, and `planted-bugs.test.ts` and `planted-closing.test.ts` with their seed still pass unchanged. |
| Tests first | All six files were written first, except the planted file's control run, which was added after the build. Before the build, 30 of 37 tests failed. The seven that passed check what must not happen, which is right before anything is sent: the answer with nothing new keeps its shape, an answer with other changes and the snapshot of a board without comments carry no key, comments on a gone subject aren't sent, a wording hidden again keeps them out, an answer received twice changes nothing, and comments never move what's on screen. After the build, all 38 pass. Review then added the session test, which failed before `session.ts` passed the re-read's comments on and passes now. |
| Not checked | No person has seen a comment arrive yet; the speech bubbles and the panel come in M3b-11. Review found that the re-read in `web/src/board/session.ts`, made when a poll changes whether the reader is an Editor or the Owner, dropped the snapshot's comments while it moved the reader's change number forward, so a comment added between the poll and the re-read never reached that reader. It now passes them on. |

## November 24 — The speech bubbles and the comments panel (D-01, A-02, I-05, M-08, J-03, I-01, H-02)

Build plan M3b-11: the board shows comments and takes new ones. The service and the shared helpers were already built (M3b-8 to M3b-10), so this changes only the web app.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| The bubbles | The prototype's speech bubble (`commentButton` in `steelman-refined.html`), with Lucide 1.17.0's message-square icon and the open count from `openCount`, is on each card between the merge handle and the sources, on each option's header for every role, and first in the question's header. It is named as the prototype names it: "Comment on this version" when empty, "1 comment on this option", "3 comments on this question". The card's bubble has a class of its own (`sm-card-comment`), not the sources' `sm-source-button`, so a card with no sources still shows no source count. An empty bubble is drawn at full strength, not at the prototype's 40%. An option's header now draws its tools for everyone; its ⋯ is still only an Editor's or the Owner's, and the cursor after an option's Undo now goes to that ⋯ by its menu role rather than to the first button in the tools. No bubble is drawn on a deleted, hidden, or moved card. |
| The panel | A bubble opens Comments in the drawer History and Settings use, one panel at a time; the bubble again, Close comments, or Escape closes it, never a click outside, and the cursor goes back to the bubble. Opening it puts the cursor in the box and rings the subject (`sm-commenting`). It quotes the subject as "Question", "Option", or "Version", lists the open threads oldest first with their replies, each with the author's initials and username ("You" for yours) and a time such as "Nov 16, 7:00 AM" with the full date in its title, and its text with the line breaks it was written with, and otherwise says "There are no comments yet." The box is "Add a comment", named "Add a comment on this version", with Comment; ⌘/Ctrl + Enter presses it. The status line says "The comments panel is open.", "Your comment was added.", and "The comments panel is closed." An empty comment is refused with "Write a comment first.", under the box and on the status line, with the box marked invalid. |
| Sending | A comment is sent at once, never queued (architecture §3), through `session.addComment`, which calls `api.ts` as Invite does. Offline, the panel says "You’re offline, so your comment wasn’t sent. It is still in the box."; at the limit, the app's too-many sentence; on a wording deleted meanwhile, "This wording was removed, so your comment wasn’t added." The text stays in the box each time. The browser keeps one ID for a draft until it's added and makes a new one when the text changes, so sending the same text again after a lost answer adds it once. A draft is kept for each subject while the board is open, through closing the panel, opening another subject, or opening History or Settings. When someone else deletes or hides the open subject, the panel closes on the next poll and the status line says "This wording was removed, so its comments are closed." When the reader deletes the wording or the option the panel is on, it closes at once, and the delete's own sentence with its Undo stays on the status line. On a closed board the box and the bubbles stay usable, since the policy table allows comments there. |
| The drawer | The drawer is now fixed to the window at every width, as Figma's and Notion's comment sidebars are: its top sits 12 px under the site's header while that is on screen, and 12 px from the window's top once it has scrolled away (`drawer.ts`). History and Settings change with it. While the Update pill shows, the drawer stops above it. At 640 px and narrower Comments covers the board, with its box at the foot of the screen. |
| Motion | Panels keep the prototype's rise as they open and now fade out as they close: React takes the drawer away at once, so `drawer.ts` keeps a copy for 160 ms with no IDs, no role, `aria-hidden`, and `inert`, so neither a screen reader nor a test finds it. A comment that arrives while the panel is open rises in and is lit for a moment, and a bubble's number rises in when it changes. With reduced motion, none of this moves and no copy is made. |
| The header on a phone | At 640 px and narrower the header's tools, now three with the bubble, sit on the sharing pill's line, and a closing pill below it may run on under them, so "Closes Nov 16 at 8 AM EST" stays on one line. The header keeps its height. |
| Share | The hint under General access gains a second sentence, "Anyone who can open it can comment." The first sentence is unchanged, so `sharing.spec.ts` stays untouched. |
| Analytics | The comment text, the quoted subject, and the box carry `ph-no-capture`. No event is added, and nothing about comments is logged. |
| Checked by machine | `app/e2e/comments.spec.ts` has 23 browser tests: each of the three bubbles opens the panel on its subject with the cursor in the box, the quote, the empty state, and the ring, and the bubble or Escape closes it with the cursor back on it and each status sentence read; a click on the board outside the panel leaves it open; the closing panel's copy comes with `aria-hidden`, no role, and no IDs and then goes, and with reduced motion no copy is made; a two-line comment keeps its line break; deleting the wording the panel is on closes it at once and keeps the Undo sentence, even after the delete is sent and polled; an empty comment is refused, and ⌘/Ctrl + Enter posts one shown as yours with the bubble's count; the other reader's count goes up on their next poll and their open panel shows the comment with its author and time, rising in (`sm-comment-new`); a Viewer can comment; a closed board accepts one and fades nothing; a draft survives closing, another subject, and History; offline, the limit, and a wording deleted meanwhile each show their sentence and keep the text; an open panel closes when the other person deletes its wording and when a moderator hides one; History and Settings close Comments and the reverse; at 1280 and 375 px a card's bubble below the fold opens the panel with its box in view, and History pressed after scrolling opens in view; the three `ph-no-capture` checks; a card with no sources has no source count and a full-strength bubble; keyboard only by name; the scan and no sideways scroll at 1280 and 375 px; and at 375 px the Owner's header with both pills, History, Settings, and the bubble has nothing overlapping, held by the new saved screenshot `comments-head-375`, taken in New York's time zone as the closing specs are, so the pill reads the same on any machine. The whole browser suite and `vitest` were run; everything else passes unchanged, apart from the two screenshots below. |
| Tests first | The first 19 tests were written first and all 19 failed, since no bubble existed. After the build all 19 pass. The review added four: the outside click, the closing copy and reduced motion, line breaks, and deleting the open subject. The line-break and delete tests failed before their fixes (a comment's text had no `white-space` rule, and the panel stayed open on a wording the reader had deleted until the delete was polled); all 23 pass now. |
| Products compared (rule 12) | Read live on Nov 24: Google Docs' help on comments (support.google.com/docs/answer/65129), whose panel lists comments beside the document and posts with Comment; GitHub's keyboard shortcuts (docs.github.com), where Command or Ctrl + Enter submits a comment. From memory: GitHub's comment box, with its placeholder in the box and Comment at its lower right, and each comment headed by the author's username and a time with the full date on hover; Google Docs keeping a draft and its phone comment view covering the document; Figma's and Notion's comment sidebars fixed to the window. Labels, order, and wording otherwise follow the prototype, with the plan's sentences. |
| Needs the builder | Two saved screenshots of the Owner's Share at 375 px no longer match, because the hint has a second sentence and the header has the bubble: `share-375` (`sharing.spec.ts`, 3% of pixels) and `share-people-375` (`people-list.spec.ts`, 3%). Replacing them is the builder's (rule 9); they were not replaced. The other saved screenshots named in the plan (board, board-viewer, card, row, and tour, and both Share ones at desktop width) still pass within 1%. The new `comments-head-375` screenshot was made locally for darwin with `--update-snapshots=missing`; its linux copy comes from CI. |
| After the full check | Comparing the 375 px Share screenshot by eye showed each person's role menu out of line: the new header rule for the closing pill matched every list item under the header, the Share panel's people rows included. The rule now applies only to the header's own items, and a new test in `comments.spec.ts` checks that the role menus share one column at 375 px; it failed before the fix and passes after. |
| The builder's approval | On Nov 24 the builder approved replacing `share-375` and `share-people-375`. The darwin copies were retaken locally and the linux copies taken from CI's Docker run, with the new `comments-head-375` linux copy. |
| Not checked | No person has commented yet. Times show as a date and time, not "5m ago" as in the prototype, because the board's answers carry no service time to measure from (E-03). Reply, Resolve, Delete, and resolved threads on the board are M3b-12's. |

## November 24 — Replies, resolving, and deleting your own comment, on the board (D-01, M-04, M-08, I-05)

Build plan M3b-12: the comments panel gains Reply, Resolve or Reopen, and Delete. The service already did all three (M3b-8 and M3b-9), so this changes only the web app, plus one note in `shared/src/comments.ts`.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| The buttons | Each comment carries the prototype's small text buttons (`commentItem` in `steelman-refined.html`), in its order: Reply on an open thread's first comment, Resolve or Reopen on the first comment, and Delete on your own. A deleted or hidden comment offers none, since the service refuses them there (`thread_deleted`). They are at least 24 px tall, scoped to the comments panel, so no other `.sm-link-button` changes. |
| Reply | Reply opens the prototype's inline form under the thread's replies, one at a time: the box "Reply", named "Your reply", with Cancel and a solid Reply, which ⌘/Ctrl + Enter presses. Escape closes the reply first and puts the cursor back on Reply; the next Escape closes the panel. Cancel does the same. An empty reply says "Write a reply first." under the box and on the status line, with the box marked invalid. A reply is sent at once with its thread's own subject, never queued; the browser keeps one ID for its draft until it's added. "Your reply was added." is read out and the cursor goes back to Reply. When the reader has typed more in the box while the reply was being sent, the form stays open with the new text, as the comment box does. Offline, the form says "You’re offline, so your reply wasn’t sent. It is still in the box." |
| A reply refused | A reply to a thread someone resolved meanwhile is refused with "This thread was resolved, so your reply wasn’t added." (architecture §3), and the draft stays. The board is read again, and the thread, now resolved, stays where it is with its form while the form is open, so the draft stays in view beside the thread's Reopen; Reopen and Reply then add it. A thread with Delete's question open stays in place the same way, with the cursor still on Cancel. A reply to a thread whose first comment was deleted meanwhile says "This comment was deleted, so your reply wasn’t added." That sentence is not in the plan's row and is new here. |
| Resolve and Reopen | Resolve folds the thread under the prototype's toggle, "1 resolved" with a chevron and `aria-expanded`, and lowers the bubble's count; the other reader's count drops on their next poll. The status line says "The thread is resolved. It is under Resolved." and the cursor goes into the comment box. The toggle shows resolved threads with the prototype's dashed edge, at full strength rather than its 70%, so their text keeps 4.5:1 contrast. Reopen says "The thread is open again." and puts the cursor in the comment box, as the row says; the prototype puts it on the thread's Resolve instead. With every thread resolved the panel says "Every comment here is resolved." above the toggle, not "There are no comments yet." |
| Delete | Delete asks first, in place of the comment's buttons: "Delete this comment?" with Delete (in the prototype's danger red) and Cancel, as a group named by its question, with the cursor on Cancel. Cancel or Escape keeps the comment and puts the cursor back on Delete. Delete says "Your comment was deleted." and puts the cursor in the comment box. A first comment with replies stays as "This comment was deleted.", with no buttons, and its replies stay; a reply, or a first comment with no replies, is removed. This is the rule `threadsOn` already had, so it is unchanged, and its note now records that M3b-12 kept it. No timed Undo is offered, since nothing brings a comment back. |
| Motion | A thread that is resolved or deleted, and a reply that is deleted, folds away rather than vanish: the panel keeps a copy for 220 ms, `aria-hidden` and `inert`, with nothing the panel looks for, while its height closes. A reopened thread rises in, as does the reply form, the question Delete asks, and resolved threads shown by the toggle. With reduced motion none of this moves and no copy is made. When someone else's change takes away the control that had the cursor, such as a thread they resolved folding away, the cursor goes into the comment box, so Escape still closes the panel. A reply form or a Delete question whose comment was deleted elsewhere is put away, and Escape then closes the panel. |
| Analytics | The reply box and each reply's text carry `ph-no-capture`. No event is added. |
| Checked by machine | `app/e2e/comment-replies.spec.ts` has 24 browser tests, with two people where the row asks. At 1280 and 375 px a reply appears under its thread for both and raises the count, with the cursor back on Reply and the other reader's reply rising in; Escape closes only the reply and the next one the panel; Cancel closes the reply; an empty reply says why; at 1280 and 375 px, Resolve folds the thread and lowers the count for both, the toggle shows it, and Reopen brings it back for both; with every thread resolved the panel says so above the toggle; only your own comments offer Delete, in the prototype's order; at 1280 and 375 px, Cancel and Escape keep the comment; when someone resolves the thread meanwhile, an open Delete question stays with the cursor on Cancel, and a focused Resolve hands the cursor to the comment box; deleting a reply removes it and the count drops for the other reader on poll; deleting a first comment with replies leaves its line, with no buttons, and the replies, for both; deleting a lone first comment brings back "There are no comments yet."; the refused reply keeps its draft through a poll, the console shows only its 409, and it is added after Reopen; offline a reply keeps its text and is added once when back; a reply that reached the service but whose answer was lost is added only once when sent again, since it carries the same ID (checked in the panel, on the bubble, and through the API); the `ph-no-capture` checks; on a closed board replying, resolving, and deleting work; keyboard only, by name; the fold's copy is hidden and goes, and with reduced motion none is made; and the scan with a reply form open, with the confirmation open, and with resolved threads shown, with no sideways scroll, at 1280 and 375 px. Each status sentence and where the cursor lands is checked in its test. The rest run at 1280 px only. `app/e2e/comments.spec.ts` (24), untouched, still passes, as do `history`, `settings`, `board-settings`, `comments-account`, `accessibility`, `closed-board`, `privacy`, and `phone` (66 in all), `web/test` (68), and `shared/test/comments.test.ts` (13). |
| Tests first | All 20 tests were written first and all 20 failed: 18 timed out waiting for Reply, Resolve, or Delete, one found no buttons on a comment, and one found no "Every comment here is resolved." line. After the build all 20 pass. The review then added four: the Resolve and Delete tests at 375 px, the lost answer, and the thread resolved meanwhile. The last failed before its fix, with the cursor lost when the focused Resolve went, and passes after. |
| Products compared (rule 12) | Read live on Nov 24: GOV.UK's Button guidance (design-system.service.gov.uk/components/button) keeps the warning button for actions people can't easily undo, asks for a confirmation step with the warning button on the final step, and shows a button group with the button first and its Cancel link after it. That gives the order here, the red Delete then Cancel, and it stays as built. Google Docs Help (support.google.com/docs/answer/65129) puts Delete in a comment's More menu, Resolve in its top corner, and Reply as a box saved with Reply, but doesn't say whether Delete asks first. GitHub Docs (Managing disruptive comments) puts Delete in a comment's ⋯ menu and leaves "deleted a comment" in the timeline, but doesn't say whether it asks first. From memory, not confirmed on those pages: Google Docs and GitHub both ask before a comment goes. Labels, order, and wording otherwise follow the prototype, with the plan's sentences. |
| By eye | The panel at 375 and 1280 px with a reply form open, with the confirmation open and resolved threads shown, and after a first comment with a reply was deleted: the buttons sit in one line under each comment, the form and the confirmation fit inside the thread with nothing cut off, and the board and the header are unchanged. |
| Not checked | No person has replied, resolved, or deleted yet. When someone deletes a thread's lone first comment while the reader's reply form is open on it, the thread goes and the draft with it. Deleting the first comment of a resolved thread leaves it resolved for good, since the service refuses to reopen it; the panel offers no Reopen there. The row's "check live" for Google Docs and GitHub is still open: their help pages, read live, don't say whether Delete asks first, and their apps need an account to try. The browser tests run in Chromium only; moving the cursor to the comment box when its control is taken away relies on the focusout Chromium sends when a focused control is removed, and isn't checked in Firefox or Safari. |

## November 24 — Links to a comment (D-01, M-06, M-04, H-02, L-01)

Build plan M3b-13: a comment gets its own address, `/b/<board>/c/<comment>`, beside the version's, and each comment gets Link. The row names no service change, but one small read was needed, described below.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| The address | `readLink` reads `/b/<board>/c/<comment>` as a board link with a `comment` key, which only a comment's link has, so `links.test.ts` is untouched and still passes. `commentPath` writes it. It is a path, so Google sign-in and the code sign-in come back to it, as they do to a version's. |
| Link | Every standing comment, replies and resolved threads included, carries Link between Resolve (or Reopen) and Delete, the prototype's place (`commentItem`). It copies the comment's address and says "Link copied.", the sentence a version's Copy link uses, with the cursor kept on Link; when copying isn't allowed it says "Copying isn’t allowed here. The link is …", as a version's does. A deleted or hidden comment has no Link, as in the prototype. |
| Opening a link | The board opens with Comments on the comment's thread, on the question, an option, or a wording. A wording's column is chosen, its row unfolded unless the wording leads it, and its card ringed but not opened, as the prototype's `openLink` does; an option's column is chosen. A resolved thread opens with the resolved threads unfolded. The comment is scrolled to the middle of the panel, ringed once (a 2 px outline that fades over 1.4 s, still for that time with reduced motion), and focused, so a screen reader reads "Comment by lin.zhao". It takes the cursor without the focus outline, as the no-access card's title does. The status line says "Opened “<question>”.", as a version's link does. A reply's thread is its first comment's. |
| The address after | The address keeps `/c/<comment>` while that comment's panel is open, so a reload lands on the same comment. Closing the panel by its button or Escape, or opening another subject's comments, puts the board's address back, or the open version's, and opening the same subject again by its bubble doesn't bring the comment's back. All changes are `replaceState`, so Back leaves the board. When the reader deletes the linked comment with its panel open, the address keeps it, as the prototype's does, and a reload then says it was deleted. |
| Notes | A deleted comment, and an ID never on the board, open the board with "That comment was deleted." and Dismiss, with the cursor on Dismiss and the address the board's. A comment whose wording was deleted or hidden, or whose option was deleted, says "That comment’s subject was removed." A deleted comment on a removed subject says it was deleted. The status line says the note, then "Opened “<question>”." |
| The service's new read | The snapshot leaves out comments whose subject is gone (M3b-10, and `comments-reading.test.ts` holds that), so a browser can't tell such a comment from one that never was. `GET /api/boards/:board/comments/:id` answers only the comment's state, `stands`, `deleted`, or `subject_removed`, never its text or author, after the same `letIn` as every board read (the existing "read" action, so `policy.ts` is unchanged). An unknown ID, a malformed one, and one from another board all read as `deleted`, with 200, so the browser logs no failed request and the answer never confirms a comment on another board. The browser asks only on a comment's link, only when the snapshot didn't send the comment, and before the board shows, so the board opens on the right note at once. No migration. |
| Checked by machine | `app/e2e/comment-links.spec.ts` has 28 browser tests. At 1280 and 375 px: Link copies the address and announces it; a link to a comment on a wording, on an option, and to a reply on the question rings and focuses it in its panel; a link to a comment on a wording in the No column that doesn't lead its row ("Library staff already work Saturdays…") picks the No tab at 375 px, unfolds the row, and rings the card, which is in view at 1280 px, so the column choice is held at both widths; a reload lands on the same comment, closing the panel gives the board's address back, and Back leaves the board; a resolved thread's link shows it; a deleted comment's link and one whose wording was deleted show their notes with the cursor on Dismiss; and the scan after arriving by a link and by a deleted one's, with no sideways scroll. At 1280 px: a link to a comment never on the board; Link on every standing comment and never on a deleted one; Escape gives the address back and a version's address still follows the open card; another subject's bubble gives the address back. Against the real service with sign-in: a comment's link to a board the person can't open shows the no-access card, and signed out, a comment's link to an invited-only board signs in first, shows nothing, then opens the comment. Two new saved screenshots, `comment-links-panel-1280` and `-375` (darwin), show a Contributor's own comment with Reply, Resolve, Link, and Delete and another's reply with Link. `app/server/test/api/comment-links.test.ts` has 9 API tests and `app/web/test/comment-links.test.ts` 13 unit tests. `comments.spec.ts`, `links.spec.ts`, `no-access.spec.ts`, `comments-account.spec.ts`, and `way-back.spec.ts` still pass, and every Vitest file (1,267 tests) passes. `comment-replies.spec.ts` has one test that now fails, listed under Needs the builder. |
| Tests first | Written first, all 24 browser tests (the screenshots aside) failed against the code before the build, run from a copy of it: the comment's address read as a board that doesn't exist, so no panel, note, or Link appeared. All 13 unit tests failed, since the address and the landing rules didn't exist. Of the 9 API tests, 7 failed on the missing route. Two passed before the build, both rightly: a board that doesn't exist answers 404, which any unknown route also did, and "the answer never carries a comment's text", which a 404 doesn't either. Two API tests then failed for faults in the tests themselves (a reply posted without its thread's target, and a signed-out call that sent the default reader), fixed in the tests. After the build all pass. |
| Products compared (rule 12) | From memory, not confirmed live: GitHub's comment ⋯ menu has "Copy link", and its address ends in `#issuecomment-<id>`; Google Docs' comment ⋮ menu has "Get link to this comment", and opening it opens the document with that comment selected. Both put the comment in the address, as this does, and both bring the comment into view when the link is opened. Checked live on Nov 24, with no answer: Google Docs' comments help (support.google.com/docs/answer/65129) and GitHub Docs' autolinked references page don't describe comment links. The label "Link", its place, and the notes follow the prototype and the plan. |
| By eye | At 1280 px the panel opens beside the board on the linked comment, ringed, with the wording's card ringed and its row unfolded; after 1.4 s the comment's ring is gone. At 375 px the panel covers the board, with the ringed comment in view; under it, the board is scrolled to the wording. The deleted note at 375 px sits under the question as the deleted version's does. The new panel screenshots show the four buttons in one line. |
| Needs the builder | `comment-replies.spec.ts` "D-01: only your own comments offer Delete, in the prototype's order after Reply and Resolve" expects exactly `["Reply", "Resolve"]`, `["Delete"]`, and `["Reply", "Resolve", "Delete"]`. With Link in the prototype's place, as the row asks, it now finds `["Reply", "Resolve", "Link"]`, `["Link", "Delete"]`, and `["Reply", "Resolve", "Link", "Delete"]`. Rule 9 leaves that test as it was; the builder decides whether to update those three lists, in a separate builder-approved commit, and M3b-13 is done only after that. With comment links built, M-06 names nothing still missing, so its status is now Built. The linux copies of the two new screenshots come from CI. |
| The builder's approval | On Nov 24 the builder approved updating M3b-12's test of each comment's buttons, in `comment-replies.spec.ts`, to include Link: `[Reply, Resolve, Link]`, `[Link, Delete]`, and `[Reply, Resolve, Link, Delete]`. It now passes, and M3b-13 is marked done. |
| Not checked | No person has followed a comment's link yet. A comment that is hidden (M3b-14) has no Link yet, as the prototype offers only Restore there. When the board is opened offline by a comment's link, the comment's state is asked once the service answers. Under 1,200 px the ringed card is under the panel. Analytics records each address change as a page view, as it already does for versions, so a comment's ID now reaches PostHog in the address; no event is added. |

## November 24 — Hiding and restoring a comment (D-01, J-02, J-03, M-02, M-04, M-06, H-03)

Build plan M3b-14: an Editor or the Owner hides someone else's comment with one of the report reasons, and restores it, with the existing `hide` action. `policy.ts` is unchanged, and its note still says it hides wordings. No migration: the comment table has had `hidden_at`, `hidden_by`, and `hidden_reason`, and the app may update them, since 0014.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| Who may | An Editor or the Owner, on someone else's comment, replies included, through `PUT` and `DELETE /api/boards/:board/comments/:id/hidden` (`moderation.ts`, beside the wording's hide). A Contributor, Voter, or Viewer is refused `not_allowed`, signed out answers 401, and a non-member 403. A closed board refuses both a hide and a restore (`board_closed`), as for a wording, so a comment posted on a closed board can't be hidden until the board is reopened. The service refuses a moderator's own comment, for a hide and a restore, with a new reason, `own_comment` (403), since `policy.ts` can't say "not the author". So an Editor whose comment the Owner hid can't restore it themselves. A comment its author deleted is refused with a new reason, `comment_deleted` (409), as its text is erased. A refusal uses no change number. |
| What each reader is sent | Both reads take the reader's moderator flag, as the wording reads do. Anyone below Editor gets a hidden comment's row with `hidden` and its reason (`hiddenReason`, left out when it isn't hidden, so a comment that isn't keeps its old shape) and no text, in the snapshot and in changes. Editors and the Owner get the text. A restore stamps the comment, so the next changes answer carries it with its text and no reason. The text stays in the database with who hid it and why (invariant 6). |
| Sending twice, and the log | A hide sent twice, even with another reason, answers `already_done` and keeps the first reason and who; a restore sent twice does the same. Each is a change row, `comment_hidden` with its reason and `comment_restored` with none, with the comment's ID as the subject and no text (invariant 7). Adding, replying, resolving, and deleting still write no row. |
| On the board | Hide sits last among the buttons on someone else's comment, after Delete, as in the prototype's `commentItem`, for an Editor or the Owner on an open board only; it is left out, not faded, for everyone else and on a closed board. Hide puts "Hide it as" with Spam, Abusive, Off-topic, Something else, and Cancel in place of the buttons, with the cursor on Cancel, and the reasons rise in as Delete's question does. Escape and Cancel put them away with the cursor back on Hide; the next Escape closes the panel. Someone else deleting or hiding the comment meanwhile, or the board closing, puts them away, and they don't come back when the comment is restored. On a thread that is already resolved, Hide's reasons and Delete's question leave the thread under the unfolded resolved toggle; only a thread that was open when they opened is held in place if someone resolves it meanwhile. A hidden comment says "A moderator removed this comment as spam." (or "as abusive.", "as off-topic.", or "A moderator removed this comment." for Something else) to everyone; an Editor or the Owner also reads its text struck through, inside `ph-no-capture`, lined up with the sentence, and Restore, its only button. A hidden comment has no Reply, Resolve, Link, or Delete. The status line says "The comment is hidden." with the cursor on Restore, and "The comment is restored." with the cursor on Hide; the session reads the board again before the panel moves the cursor. A comment hidden or restored while the panel is open fades its new text in once; with reduced motion it doesn't. |
| History | History says "maya.chen hid a comment as spam." and "maya.chen restored a comment.", with "a comment" a link to the comment's address when the reader holds it and its thread can open; pressing it closes History and opens Comments on the comment, focused and ringed, as its link does. It never shows the comment's words, and it offers no Restore for a comment, which has its own in the panel. |
| Links | A hidden comment's link lands on it, in its panel, where its sentence shows; the service's link read answers `stands` for it. |
| The simulation | Hide and restore of a comment are steps, Ana's alone, and refused on a closed board. Their results are checked against the database in the write path's order (`not_allowed`, `board_closed`, `comment_deleted`, `own_comment`, then done or already done), sent twice, and counted apart from the wordings' hides. A new always-true check, "10. No hidden comment's text reaches anyone but an Editor or the Owner", runs on every snapshot and every changes answer. A planted read that sends a hidden comment's text to everyone is caught by it, and a planted service that lets a moderator hide their own comment is caught by check 9. |
| Checked by machine | `app/e2e/comment-hiding.spec.ts` has 13 browser tests: at 1280 and 375 px, two people, the hide, the sentence for the other reader on the next poll, the restore, the status sentences and the cursor; each reason's sentence; who is offered Hide and Restore, and neither on a closed board; Escape and Cancel; the reasons put away when the comment is deleted meanwhile (a reply keeps its thread in place), when another moderator hides it and then restores it, and when the board closes; Hide on a resolved thread's comment, which stays under the toggle with the cursor on Cancel and then on Restore; History's two entries and its link; a hidden comment's link; and the scan, with the reasons open (once they have risen in) and a hidden comment, for an Editor and a Contributor, at 1280 and 375 px, with no sideways scroll. `app/server/test/api/comment-hiding.test.ts` has 13 API tests; the change log's check first finds the two comment entries, then that they hold no text. `app/server/test/simulation/comment-hiding.test.ts` has 1 fixed sequence with exact counts, and `planted-comment-hiding.test.ts` 3 tests. `app/web/test/comment-hiding.test.ts` has 5 unit tests: the re-read on a role change takes a hidden comment's text away from a reader who stops being an Editor and gives it to one who becomes one, a restored comment loses its reason, and Hide and Restore say what they did or why not. Every file in the simulation folder (20 tests, the 300 and 1,000 random sequences included), every server API, database, and shared test (1,106), and every web unit test (86) pass. `comments.spec.ts`, `comment-links.spec.ts`, `comments-account.spec.ts`, `history.spec.ts`, `moderation.spec.ts`, `closed-board.spec.ts`, and `accessibility.spec.ts` pass; `comment-replies.spec.ts` passes except the test already listed under Needs the builder for M3b-13, which this item doesn't change, as Hide never shows for a Contributor. |
| Tests first | Written first, all 13 API tests failed (404, no route) and the first 10 browser tests failed against the board before the build. Of the three added after review, the hidden-then-restored and board-closing cases fail with the panel's check that puts the reasons away turned off, and the resolved-thread case fails with every asked-about thread held in place; the deleted case passes either way, since a deleted comment shows no reasons. The simulation tests and the web unit tests were written after the service and the session, so they passed when first run; the two planted tests show the new check, and check 9, fail with the bug in place. |
| Products compared (rule 12) | The prototype's `commentItem`: Hide last, the reasons and Cancel inline in place of the buttons, Restore the only button on a hidden comment, its text struck through for editors. The board's own wording menu: the group name "Hide it as" and the reasons' labels. From memory, not confirmed live: GitHub's comment ⋯ menu has "Hide", which asks for a reason (Spam, Abuse, Off topic, and others), folds the comment to a line saying it was hidden and why, and offers "Unhide" to moderators; this follows it with a reason first, a sentence in the comment's place, and Restore. The sentence replaces the prototype's "Removed by a moderator · Spam", as the plan asks, since every line read is a full sentence (rule 13). |
| By eye | At 1280 and 375 px, as an Editor, the hidden comment shows the grey sentence over the struck text, both at the comment's indent, with Restore under them; the reasons wrap to a second line after Off-topic in the panel's width, at the same indent. As a Contributor the hidden comment shows only the sentence. History's entry reads "maya.chen hid a comment as spam." with "a comment" underlined. |
| Needs the builder | Nothing for this item. The pending decision on `comment-replies.spec.ts` (PR 124) is unchanged, since that test opens the board as a Contributor. |
| Not checked | No person has hidden a comment yet. Reporting a comment isn't built: the report table is keyed by wording, so it needs a table of its own. A comment its author deleted after it was hidden stays marked hidden, and reads as deleted. An author can still delete their own hidden comment by the API, which erases it. A hide or a restore moves the board's last-activity time on the board list through its change row, as a wording's does; no test checks that for a comment. Download your data still gives an author their own hidden comment's text, since they wrote it. Migration 0014's note "Comments aren't in the change log" is out of date for hide and restore; rule 7 leaves it as it is. |

## November 24 — The app's icon (M-05)

Build plan M3d-1: the app gets the honeycomb mark as its tab icon, a touch icon for a phone's home screen, and a theme colour for the browser's own bar. Only `app/web/index.html` and one new image, `app/web/src/apple-touch-icon.png`, change. The service, its Content Security Policy, and the docs pages stay as they are.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| The tab icon | `index.html` carries the mark from `docs/design/logo.svg` as an inline `data:image/svg+xml` icon, with the docs pages' square box and encoding. The SVG holds its own `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` rule, so the mark is `--sm-ink` (#242424) on a light tab and `--sm-card` (#fdfdfd) on a dark one. No file sits at the site root, because `serveWebApp` answers every path outside `/assets/` with the page. |
| The touch icon | `web/src/apple-touch-icon.png` is 180 px, the mark in `--sm-ink` on `--sm-paper` (#dcd8cf), with no transparency, since iOS fills a transparent corner with black. It is linked from `index.html`, so Vite writes it as `/assets/apple-touch-icon-<hash>.png` and the service keeps it a year. It was made once by rendering `logo.svg` in Playwright's Chromium at 180 px and saving it as RGB with Pillow. No package was added. |
| The theme colour | Two `theme-color` metas: `--sm-card` (#fdfdfd) under a light system theme and `--sm-ink` (#242424) under a dark one. tokens.css has no dark palette, so the dark value reuses the ink. The app itself has no dark theme, so on a phone in dark mode the browser's bar is dark above a light page. |
| The server | Checked, not changed: `contentSecurityPolicy` in `app.ts` already has `img-src 'self' data: blob:`, and `serveWebApp` in `web.ts` already serves `.png` as `image/png` under `/assets/` with `public, max-age=31536000, immutable`. |
| Checked by machine | `app/web/test/app-icon.test.ts` has 5 tests. It builds the web app with Vite into a temporary folder and reads the built page: exactly one link whose `rel` names an icon, so a second `shortcut icon` or `alternate icon` fails it; its SVG is square and holds all four of `logo.svg`'s polygons and the dashed edge; Playwright's Chromium parses the SVG with `DOMParser` with no parse error and an `<svg>` root, and draws it as an image; every polygon fills with `currentColor` or nothing and strokes with `currentColor`, and the only colours in the SVG are the two tokens; the light colour is `--sm-ink` and the dark rule inside the SVG's `<style>` is `--sm-card`, read from tokens.css; one touch icon under `/assets/`, a 180 by 180 RGB PNG; and two theme colours with their media queries, matching tokens.css. `app/server/test/api/app-icon.test.ts` has 2 tests. It builds the web app the same way and serves it as production does: the touch icon's `/assets/` address answers 200 with `image/png`, the year-long cache header, and a PNG, and the page's Content Security Policy still allows `data:` images. `deployed.test.ts`, `security-headers.test.ts`, and `smoke.test.ts`, untouched, still pass, and the whole Vitest run, 121 files and 1252 tests including these 7, passes. The browser tests in `accessibility`, `analytics`, `sign-out`, `card-details`, `comments`, and `new-steelman` (94), three of them with saved screenshots, still pass unchanged. `npm run typecheck` and ESLint on the new files pass. |
| Tests first | All 7 tests were written first and all 7 failed on the bare page: the web tests found no icon link, no touch icon, and no theme colour, and the first server test found no touch icon address. The second server test failed only on the page having no icon link; its `data:` check alone would have passed, which is right, since the policy already allowed it. After the build all 7 pass. After review, the web tests also put the icon through Chromium's parser and check each polygon's colour. A hard-coded fill on one polygon fails the colour test, and a duplicated attribute fails the parse test and the colour test. |
| Products compared (rule 12) | Read live on Nov 24 with curl: github.com's page has an SVG `rel=icon` (`favicons/favicon.svg`), a PNG `alternate icon`, a mask icon, and one `theme-color` (#1e2327). Its favicon.svg fills the mark #24292E and holds no `@media` rule; a separate `favicon-dark.svg` fills it white, and the link's `js-site-favicon` class and `data-base-href` let a script swap the file when the system theme changes. Taken from GitHub: an SVG icon whose mark is dark on a light tab and white on a dark one. Here the swap is a `@media` rule inside the SVG rather than a script, since the app's policy allows no inline script. google.com's page, read live, has no icon link and relies on `/favicon.ico`, the full-colour G, which doesn't change with the theme. From memory, not confirmed: Chrome and Firefox apply `prefers-color-scheme` inside an SVG favicon, and Safari doesn't show SVG favicons in its tabs. |
| By eye | Headless Chromium draws no tab strip, so the icon's data URI was drawn in an `<img>` at 16, 24, 32, and 64 px on a tab-bar grey and in a mock tab, with Playwright's `colorScheme` set to light and then dark. Light: the mark is near black, the filled hexagon solid, the two outlined ones clear. Dark: the same mark turns near white on the dark grey, with nothing left dark. At 16 px the dashed fourth hexagon reads as a fainter grey outline rather than separate dashes; from 24 px up the dashes show. The touch icon at 180 px shows the four hexagons centred on the warm paper colour with room around them for the rounded mask. |
| Needs the builder | Look at the mark on a light tab and on a dark tab in a real browser. Say whether the dark browser bar above the light app is wanted, or whether the dark `theme-color` should also be a light colour. |
| Not checked | The real tab icon in a browser's tab strip, and the touch icon on an iPhone's home screen; the tab is checked on staging after the merge (rule 14). Safari, Firefox, and Android are not checked. The touch icon is a committed image, not made by a script, so a change to the ink or paper token needs it drawn again by hand. The docs pages keep their icon without the dark rule, as the row says. |

## November 24 — A motion system: a settle curve, play, FLIP, and wires that follow (M-15)

Build plan M3d-2: the foundation the later motion rows build on. It adds tokens, one small module, and a redraw loop for the wires, and it turns three timings in the code into tokens with the same values. Nothing calls `flip()` yet, so nothing on screen moves differently. The service and `shared/` don't change.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| The tokens | `docs/design/tokens.css` gains `--sm-settle`, a short spring written with CSS `linear()`. It is sampled from a damped spring (damping ratio 0.72) that passes its mark by 4 percent at the halfway point and comes to rest by the end. Its comment records that the builder approved it on Nov 24, although the brief had one curve with no overshoot. `--sm-lift` is the shadow a moving card wears, between a card's hover shadow and a floating panel's. `--sm-leave` (160 ms), `--sm-fold` (220 ms), and `--sm-ring` (1400 ms) hold the timings that were in the code. `--sm-fast`, `--sm-medium`, `--sm-slow`, and `--sm-ease` are unchanged. Lines 32 and 82 of `docs/design/README.md` now describe two curves, the ease and the settle, and the lift. `python3 scripts/sync-prototype.py` was run, and only the token block changed in `docs/prototype/index.html`. |
| `motion.ts` | `web/src/board/motion.ts` ports the prototype's `play()` and the FLIP in its `render()`. `reducedMotion()` reads matchMedia. `play()` returns null and starts nothing under reduced motion; otherwise it plays at `--sm-medium` on `--sm-ease` unless told otherwise, and a cancelled animation's rejected `finished` is caught, so it never shows as a page error. `captureRects()` records the viewport rect of each row (`[data-node]`) and card (`.sm-card[data-card]`), or of whatever a caller's selector names, and skips hidden elements. `flip()` cancels its own earlier move on each element (never a CSS animation), measures, and moves each element from its old offset to `none` at `--sm-slow` on `--sm-settle`, with `fill: "backwards"` so that it holds its old place while it waits and holds nothing at the end. A card in a moving row moves only by the difference, so it doesn't travel twice as far. An element that moved less than half a pixel gets no animation. The stagger is 20 ms in reading order, top to bottom and then left to right along a line, and stops growing after 12 turns, so the last row on a long board starts within 240 ms. Rows and cards are matched by their argument and version IDs, so an element React draws anew moves from the old one's place. Elements with no earlier rect are returned as arrivals. With `lift`, the card that moves wears `--sm-lift` as it starts and is set down in its own shadow, on the move's timing. When a row moves, the shadow goes on the row's card (`.sm-card-wrap > .sm-card`), never on the row, which holds the vote column too; a card that also moves within its row lifts on its own move, once. A second `flip()` cancels the lift with the move. `flip()` returns `{ arrivals, animations }` rather than the row's bare list of arrivals, because `redrawWhile()` needs the animations, and `motion.ts` also exports `duration(name, from)` and `BOARD_MOVERS`, which goes beyond the row's four functions. M3d-3, M3d-4, and the later rows use this signature. The settle curve falls back to the ease in a browser that can't read `linear()`. Tokens are read when they're used, never when the module loads. |
| Wires | `Wires.tsx` gains `redrawWhile(animations)`. Web Animations change no attribute and no size, so the existing observers don't see a move. It redraws the wires on every animation frame until every move finishes or is cancelled, as the prototype's `animateWires()` does, and then redraws the wires and textures once at the final layout. It uses no clock, only `requestAnimationFrame` and each animation's `finished`. While a move runs, the textures aren't repainted, so they stay static. The header comment now says so. |
| Timings as tokens | The drawer's fade (`app.css`, `drawer.ts`), a thread's fold (`app.css`, `Comments.tsx`), and a link's ring (`Board.tsx`) read `--sm-leave`, `--sm-fold`, and `--sm-ring`. `drawer.ts`, `Comments.tsx`, and `Board.tsx` read them through `duration()`, which falls back to the old values when a token is missing. `app.css` reads `var(--sm-leave)` and `var(--sm-fold)` directly. `drawer.ts` and `Comments.tsx` use `reducedMotion()` from the new module. |
| Checked by machine | `app/web/test/motion.test.ts` has 15 tests. Twelve run in Node on fake elements: `play()` under reduced motion and without it, the moves and the still element, the card in a moving row, a second flip mid-flight, arrivals and hidden elements, an element drawn anew, the stagger's reading order, the stagger's cap, the lift on a card that moves on its own, the lift on a moving row's card (and not on the row), the fallback to the ease, and reading the timing tokens. Three run the module in Playwright's Chromium, built with Vite as `web/test/app-icon.test.ts` does, on a column of four rows with the real tokens: reversing them plays four moves on the settle curve with no card moving on its own, and four lifts on the rows' cards and none on the rows, each ending at the reversed layout; a first reversal is paused 150 ms in, in the same step, and the second reversal then starts row a from a place strictly between its old and new ones, ending at the new layout with no page error; and under reduced motion nothing animates and the layout is the same. `app/e2e/motion-system.spec.ts` has 3 browser tests on the demo board: the new tokens reach `#steelman-study` (the settle curve is `linear()` that Chromium accepts, starts at 0, peaks between 1.03 and 1.05, and ends at 1; the lift is a valid shadow; the six timings read 120, 200, 420, 160, 220, and 1400 ms; the ease is unchanged); the drawer's fade and a thread's fold still compute to 0.16 s and 0.22 s on the ease; and under reduced motion `document.getAnimations()` stays empty while a row unfolds and folds and Comments opens and closes. The existing browser tests most likely to be affected, untouched, still pass: `accessibility` (with the reduced-motion test), `wires-texture`, `comments`, `comment-replies`, `links`, and `screen` (90), and every spec with saved screenshots, `card-details`, `list`, `phone`, `roles-on-screen`, `new-steelman`, `people-list`, `sign-in`, `sharing`, and `tour` (120). No saved screenshot changed or was replaced. The machine was under heavy load (load average 13), and 10 of those tests first timed out at 30 s, each in a wait or an accessibility scan and none in a screenshot comparison. The 4 accessibility tests passed when rerun with motion-system. The other 6 passed when their specs were rerun on one worker, except the desktop Viewer scan, which passed alone with a 120 s timeout. The first browser run showed that the build's CSS minifier writes `120ms` as `.12s`, so `duration()` reads seconds as well as milliseconds, and the browser test now compares milliseconds. `web/test` (88 tests, these 15 included) passes. `npm run typecheck` and ESLint on `web/src/board` and the new files pass. |
| Tests first | All 14 unit tests were written first and failed, because `motion.ts` didn't exist. Of the 3 browser tests, the token test failed (`--sm-settle` read as empty), and the other two passed before the build. That is right: they guard what the build must keep, the fade and fold durations and the absence of Web Animations under reduced motion. After the build all 17 pass. One unit test failed at first because its own data left a middle row in place, and it was corrected. A review then found that a lifted row wore the shadow as a square box around the whole row, vote column included, while its card wore none, and that the Chromium mid-flight test could run its second reversal after the first had finished. The row-lift unit test and the card-only lift checks in Chromium were added and failed against the earlier `flip()` (4 of 15 failed), and the mid-flight test now pauses the first reversal in the same step and checks where the second starts. After the fix all 18 pass. |
| Products compared (rule 12) | Apple's Human Interface Guidelines on motion, read live on Nov 24 through developer.apple.com's JSON for the page, ask for purposeful, brief motion that follows people's expectations, that can be interrupted, and that never carries information alone. That is why `flip()` can be interrupted by the next change and still end at the final layout, and why reduced motion removes the moves without losing anything. Material Design 3's page on easing and duration didn't load without JavaScript, so it comes from memory and isn't confirmed: its standard easing is a quick start and gentle stop close to `--sm-ease`, its durations put a move like this one between about 300 and 500 ms (`--sm-slow` is 420 ms), and its newer spring tokens allow a small overshoot for spatial motion, which is what `--sm-settle` adds. No screen gains a control or a sentence, so rule 13 has nothing to check. |
| Needs the builder | Nothing for this row. The settle curve can be seen once M3d-3 uses it on Update. |
| Not checked | Nothing on the board calls `flip()` or `redrawWhile()` yet, so wires following moving cards is checked first by M3d-3's browser test. Safari and Firefox are not checked; `linear()` needs Safari 17.2 or Firefox 112, and older browsers get the ease. The settle curve hasn't been watched by a person on a real board. |

## November 25 — Cards shuffle into the new order on Update (B-05, M-02, M-15)

Build plan M3d-3: when the reader presses Update, the rows that moved slide from where they were into the new order, while the row being read stays still and the wires stay on their cards. Only `web/src/board/Board.tsx` and `web/src/board/motion.ts` change. The service, `shared/`, the prototype's CSS, and the saved screenshots stay as they are.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| What was built | `update()` in `Board.tsx` now takes `captureRects()` of the scene next to `readingAnchor()`, before `session.update()`, and keeps them in a ref that only Update sets. The layout effect keyed on `view.shown` clears that ref as it clears the anchor. When Update left rects behind, it first stops any shuffle still running with the new `stopMoves()`, then keeps the anchored row still with its `window.scrollBy` as before, and only then calls `flip(before, scene, { lift: true, travel: true })` and hands its animations to `redrawWhile()`. The view's order also changes on polls, deletions, and restructures, but none of those set the ref, so other people's votes still move nothing (M-02). `flip()` gains an opt-in `travel` option: a moving row runs from its old offset at scale 1, through 1.01 halfway, to `none` at scale 1, in one animation with the move, and a row moving toward the top is raised one above its resting z-index on the same keyframes, so it passes over a row moving down and holds nothing after the end. Without `travel`, `flip()`'s keyframes are unchanged. `stopMoves(root)` cancels the moves `flip()` started under the root and is what `flip()` itself now uses first. The new `drawnRect()` measures an element where it's drawn but at its own size: for a row still growing in a shuffle, it divides out the row's computed `scale` about the rect's centre and keeps the move. `captureRects()` and `readingAnchor()` both use it, so a second Update starts each row centred where it's seen, at scale 1, and holds the row being read without the half-pixel-to-few-pixel shift the growth would add. Timings and curves are still the tokens `--sm-slow`, `--sm-settle`, and `--sm-lift`. No CSS, no text, and no package were added, and the status after Update is still "Board updated." |
| Checked by machine | `app/e2e/shuffle-update.spec.ts` has 6 browser tests, 3 at 1280 px and 3 at 375 px, each with two people. In the first, tess.b votes "Opening…" up in her browser and two more people vote through the API, so the next shared order puts it above "Staff…". After the poll and the next tick the pill reads "1 change Update", and the rows' rects are unchanged and no Web Animation runs on any row or card. The test then presses Update inside the page and holds the new animations paused, so the frames can be set exactly, however slow the machine is. At the first frame every row is drawn within 1 px of its place before Update. At 0, 15, 30, 50, and 75 percent of the shuffle, the anchored row is within 1 px of its place and every wire's ends are within 2 px of their question, header, or card. Each moved row's move has a keyframe at `scale: "1.01"` and offset 0.5, and each moved row, and no other, has a lift animation on its card. After the moves finish, each row that moved has exactly one animation on the row itself, a row that stayed and anything in it has none, the anchored row is within 1 px, the wires are within 1 px, the No order is "Opening…", "Staff…", "Few…", and every row's place on the page matches a reload's within 1 px. At 1280 px the anchor is "Sunday…" and only "Opening…" and "Staff…" move. At 375 px only No shows, the anchor is "Staff…", and the page scrolls to hold it, so "Opening…" and "Few…" move on screen. The second test holds the first shuffle 20 percent in, where the settle curve has the moving rows at their largest, brings a newer order that puts "Staff…" back on top, and presses Update again: every animation of the first shuffle is cancelled, every row's centre in the second shuffle's first frame is within 1 px of where the paused first shuffle drew it, the anchor stays within 1 px, the order is the newest one, the wires are in place, and the layout matches a reload's. The third runs Update under reduced motion: `document.getAnimations()` is empty, the anchor stays within 1 px, and the layout matches a reload's. `app/web/test/shuffle-motion.test.ts` has 6 tests that run `motion.ts` in Playwright's Chromium: with `travel`, each row's keyframes are the move with scale 1, 1.01, and 1, and its first frame is its old place at its own size; where a row moving up and a row moving down overlap, `elementFromPoint` finds the one moving up, raised to z-index 2 while the other stays at 1, and after the end every row is back on z-index 1 in the final layout; without `travel` the keyframes are the plain move; `stopMoves()` cancels every move with no page error; a second flip 100 ms into a traveling reversal, when every row is drawn wider than its 1280 px, measures each row at 1280 by 88 and starts each one centred within 0.5 px of where it was drawn, at scale 1; and under reduced motion `travel` starts nothing. The existing tests that press Update or guard motion, untouched, still pass: `live-changes`, `wires-texture`, `combine`, `m3a-audit`, `motion-system`, `accessibility`, `screen`, `phone`, and `tour` (69). Every other spec with saved screenshots, `card-details`, `roles-on-screen`, `comments`, `list`, `new-steelman`, `people-list`, `sharing`, and `sign-in`, still passes (127), and no saved screenshot changed or was replaced. `web/test` (14 files, 94 tests, including `motion.test.ts`'s 15) passes. All of these, the 69, the 127, and the 94, were run again on Nov 25 against the final code, with `drawnRect()` in place, and pass. `npm run typecheck` and ESLint on the changed files pass. The simulation was not run, since this row doesn't touch the session or the server. |

## November 25 — Cards arrive, and your deleted version gives up its place (A-03, A-05, A-06, J-05, M-15)

Build plan M3d-4: the argument or wording you add rises in, the slot after it makes room, your deleted version leaves through a copy of its card, and Undo plays that exit backwards. Only `web/src/board/Board.tsx` and `web/src/app.css` change, with a new `web/src/board/Leaving.tsx`. The service, `shared/`, `motion.ts`, the prototype's CSS, and the saved screenshots stay as they are.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| What was built | `writer.addArgument` and `writer.addWording` in `Board.tsx` note the new card's version ID, which the browser makes, with where its column or its row's track is and what comes after it (the column's Add an argument slot, or the track's Suggest a better version card). A layout effect that runs after every render notes where those slots are, relative to their column or track. When the card with that ID is first drawn, which is after the add is sent and polled, it plays the prototype's `render(..., 'added')` entrance, `translateY(8px)` and opacity 0 to none and 1 at `--sm-medium` on `--sm-ease`, through `play()`, and the slot moves from its noted place with `flip()` scoped to that column or track, so Update's shuffle is never cancelled. Both go to `redrawWhile()`. An add the queue stops holding without it arriving (refused) is forgotten. Other people's additions are never noted, so they still join the end at once. When you delete your version, `copyOf()` clones its card as drawn, drops every id, data, and ARIA attribute, and places it in a new layer of the scene that is `aria-hidden` and `inert`, as Comments.tsx's folding thread is. The copy's wording fades and its height eases into the dashed placeholder, with "Deleted by" and the trash icon drawn as Card.tsx's placeholder is, on `--sm-fold`, and then it fades on `--sm-leave` and leaves the page when its animations finish, with a timer a second later as a backstop. Each animation rests at the copy's gone state, so nothing flashes before it's removed. The board takes its layout without the card at once, exactly as before (`leaveOut()` is unchanged), so the rows below take their places at once and nothing animates on them, and the Undo bar is where it was. Undo of a delete removes any copy still leaving and, once the card is drawn again, plays `comeBack()`: the card grows from the placeholder's strip at its top to its full height with a clip-path while its wording fades in, on `--sm-fold`, ending with no clip. Focus and every sentence are unchanged. Under reduced motion no copy is drawn and nothing plays. The layer sits under the board's cards, so the card or Undo bar that takes the deleted card's place stays on top while the copy fades behind it. |
| Checked by machine | `app/e2e/cards-arrive-leave.spec.ts` has 13 browser tests, 6 at each of 1280 and 375 px and one at 1280. Adding an argument: the click is made in the page, and the animations that start are caught and paused. There is exactly one entrance on the new card, with the prototype's keyframes at 200 ms, and exactly one move on the column's add slot, from a negative y offset to `none`, and nothing else. At its first frame the card's opacity is 0. After it ends, the No order is the three rows and the new one, and every row's and card's rect on the page matches a reload's within 1 px. Adding a wording: one entrance on the new card, one move on the track's add card from a negative x offset to `none`, nothing else, the track lists the three wordings, and after unfolding the row again on a reload every rect matches within 1 px. Halfway through the fold, where the copy and the Undo bar that took the row's place overlap, the Undo bar is drawn above the copy: the test lets the layer take the cursor for that one look, and `document.elementsFromPoint()` lists the bar before the copy. At the end of the fold its height equals its note's height plus its border within 1 px, its wording is at opacity 0, its border is dashed, and it says "Deleted by jordan_w". The row below doesn't move while the copy leaves. After it ends the copy is gone, and once the delete is sent and polled nothing animates and the layout matches a reload's. Other people's new argument and wording, through the API, join the end at once with no Web Animation running and none started. Both tests wrap `Element.prototype.animate` to count every animation the page starts, so one that starts and ends between two checks still counts. The new spec passed twice in a row (`--repeat-each=2`, 26 runs). The existing specs most likely to be affected, untouched, still pass: `card-menu`, `live-changes`, `phone`, `accessibility`, `closed-board`, `shuffle-update`, `motion-system`, `strongest-case`, `comments`, `options`, `history-additions`, `history-undo`, `combine`, and `m3a-audit` (148), and `screen`, `roles-on-screen`, `card-details`, `wires-texture`, `tour`, `comment-links`, `list`, `sharing`, `new-steelman`, `people-list`, and `sign-in` (157).
