## November 25 — Rows unfold and fold, and card details open and close (A-04, A-02, M-15)

Build plan M3d-5: Compare unfolds a row with motion, Fold folds it back, and a card's details ease open and shut. Only `web/src/board/Board.tsx` and `web/src/app.css` change, with a new `web/src/board/unfold.ts`. The service, `shared/`, `motion.ts`, `Card.tsx`, `Track.tsx`, the prototype's CSS, and the saved screenshots stay as they are.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| What was built | Compare and Fold call `beforeToggle()` in the column's toggle, before the row's state changes. It notes the board's `gridTemplateColumns` as drawn, each toggled row's height, and its lead card's ID and width, and it copies the folding row's versions. A layout effect then calls `playToggle()` once React has drawn the new state. It measures every final value first, then plays through `play()`: the board's columns ease from the old template to the one `boardColumns()` gives, on `--sm-slow`, only where they change (a phone's board has one column); the lead card's width eases from what it was; the unfolding row's `.sm-track-axis` grows from a height and margin of 0. Each version after the lead, its vote with it, then slides from the lead card's x, measured in that first frame, to its place, 45 ms after the one before (the prototype's `Math.floor(index / 2) * 45`, capped at 270 ms, six turns after the first), clipped to the right of the lead card so that it comes out from under it. Each toggled row starts taking the room it took as drawn, through its bottom margin, so the rows below ease into place. That room includes any height and margin still moving from a toggle before, and a row still moving from one is included too, so a second click never makes the rows below jump. On Fold the row closes at once, as today, so every control, test, and screen reader sees the folded row. The copy of its versions, with every id, data, and ARIA attribute dropped, sits in a new `.sm-folding-layer` of the scene that is `aria-hidden` and `inert`, in a box where the track was that clips them as the track did. Each copy slides to the lead card's x and fades on `--sm-medium`, as the prototype's `foldOut` does, while the row gives its axis's room back, and then the copy leaves the page when its animations finish, with a timer a second later as a backstop. On a fold alone, the board's columns, the lead card's width, and the row's height wait `--sm-medium` and hold their first frame until the copy is home, as the prototype awaits `foldOut()` before its `render(..., 'fold')`; when another row unfolds at the same time, they move at once. A card's wording and chevron call `holdReveal()` before the card's state changes, which copies details that are closing. `playReveal()` then grows the opened `.sm-card-reveal` from a height of 0 on `--sm-medium`, as the prototype's `'detail'` does. The real reveal of a closing card is `hidden` at once, exactly as today, and its inert, `aria-hidden` copy is placed beside it and eases from its height to 0, as `collapseOut` does, then leaves. Every animation goes to `redrawWhile()`. None holds its end, so each ends in today's layout. Update calls `stopRowMotion()` before it measures. Only the reader's own Compare, Fold, wording, and chevron animate; the tour, a link, History, Strongest case, and a suggestion open rows and cards at once, as before. Focus and the status line are unchanged, and no sentence is added. Under reduced motion nothing plays and no copy is drawn. Unlike the prototype, nothing a screen reader or a contrast check reads fades, since a half-faded wording would fail the check; only the copies fade. |

Compare, with the Sunday row given four wordings through the API: the click is made in the page and the animations are caught and paused. At 1280 there is one animation on the board, whose first and last `gridTemplateColumns` differ, at 420 ms; at 375 there is none on the board. There is one width animation on the lead card with different ends, and the axis grows from `0px`. Every version after the lead, and its vote, slides from a negative `translateX` to `none` at 420 ms, 0, 45, and 90 ms late in turn, and the lead's vote doesn't move. Every caught animation is on the board, the axis, the row, its lead card, its other versions and votes, or its track's new-wording slot, and on nothing else. At the first frame every version's left edge is within 1 px of the lead card's. After it ends, no copy is left, an accessibility scan passes, and every row's and card's rect matches a reduced-motion run of the same within 1 px, which starts no animation. Fold: the row has no track and shows its Compare button at once; the layer is `aria-hidden="true"` and `inert` and holds three copied cards with no id, tabindex, data, or ARIA attribute; each copy runs from `none` at opacity 1 to opacity 0, and its slide ends within 1 px of the lead card's x and starts at once; the lead's width, the row's bottom margin, and at 1280 the board's columns ease after a 200 ms delay, and the row's axis room shrinks at once. At the first frame each copy is within 1 px of where its version was. After it ends, the copy is gone, an accessibility scan passes, and the layout matches the folded board's within 1 px. Closing sets `hidden` on the real reveal at once, removes the Context region from the accessibility tree, and places one copy that is `aria-hidden`, `inert`, and without an id, which runs from its height to `0px` and is that tall at the first frame. The focus stays on the chevron and the status line doesn't change, and after it ends the copy is gone, the reveal is still hidden, and the layout matches the closed card's within 1 px. Focus and speech: pressing Enter on Compare, the lead's wording, its chevron, and Fold leaves the focus and the status line, after each, exactly where a reduced-motion run leaves them. On a row with ten wordings (1280 only), the nine versions after the lead start 0, 45, ... 270 ms late, and the last three all at 270 ms. Under reduced motion, Compare, opening and closing a card, and Fold start no Web Animation at all (`Element.prototype.animate` is counted) and draw no copy. The saved row-open, card-open, card-form, tour, and board screenshots still match; none was replaced. `web/test` (16 files, 112 tests) passes. `npm run typecheck` and ESLint on the changed files pass. ## November 25 — Combining and separating settle in place (A-10, J-05, M-15)

Build plan M3d-7, as the builder answered question 7 on Nov 24: cards move only on Update. So the row's conditional applies: the absorbed wording's fade, the stack count's scale, and a new row's 8 px rise stay, and the rows below take their places at once, as they do today, with no `flip()` on them. A new `web/src/board/settle.ts` holds the motion. `web/src/board/session.ts` gains `onMove()`, `web/src/board/Board.tsx` calls it, and `web/src/app.css` gains the settling layer. `motion.ts` now exports `curve()`, and `unfold.ts` exports `strip()` and `place()`, with no change to what they do. The service, `shared/`, `policy.ts`, the combine dialog and its sentences, and the saved screenshots stay as they are.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| What was built | `session.ts` calls the board's `onMove()` listener twice for the reader's own combine, separation, Undo on the bar, and Undo in History. The first call comes as the change is sent, while the screen still shows the wording where it was. The second comes just before the session sets the view that `showMoved()` or `putBack()` gives, with that view. The poll that shows a move whose read had failed makes the second call too. A change the service refuses gets only the first call, and nothing plays. Other people's combines and Update never call it. On the first call React first draws what the session has just changed, through `flushSync()`: an earlier move's Undo bar that goes, and the dialog that closes. Then `beforeSettle()` notes, where the reader sees the board next, which rows are drawn, the row the wording is in, and a copy of its card. The copy is taken then because the read after the change already draws a row combined away as the combined-away row, without its card. On the second call `readySettle()` copies the card again if it's still drawn, so that the copy starts from where it's seen, measured with `drawnRect()` during a shuffle. A layout effect then calls `playSettle()` once the session holds the new view. Three things can play, each inside one column. First, when the wording is combined away into a row already drawn, by a combine or by Undo of a separation, the copy of its card fades from opacity 1 to 0 on `--sm-medium` where the card was, and then it leaves the page, with a timer a second later as a backstop. A separated wording leaves no copy, because its new row rises in instead. Second, each stack drawn before and after whose count changed plays one `scale` from 1.12, the prototype's hover size, back to 1 on `--sm-slow` and `--sm-settle`. That is the row the wording left and the row it joined. The `scale` property is used, so the count's `translate` and its hover `transform` are untouched. Third, a row that wasn't drawn before, such as a separated wording's row or a row that Undo brings back, rises 8 px and fades in at its place on `--sm-medium`. When the wording joins a row that already exists and its card is drawn there, the card rises in the same way. No row below plays anything, and no keyframe moves anything sideways. The copy goes in a new `.sm-settling-layer` of the scene, which is `aria-hidden`, `inert`, and `ph-no-capture`, and the copy has no id, data, or ARIA attribute. Its wording's `sm-claim` class is swapped for its look, frozen inline, so that no lookup of a card by its wording finds the copy. The layer is drawn over the rows (z-index 2), unlike M3d-4's delete copy, because the board takes its new layout at once and the row below takes the card's place, so a copy under it would be hidden. The copy and what took its place cross-fade. Update and a row's Compare or Fold call `stopSettling()` before they measure. Update also forgets the screen a move still being sent was sent from, so that the move plays from the screen Update drew and copies no card from where it was before. Every animation goes through `play()` and to `redrawWhile()`, and none holds its end. Under reduced motion no copy is made and nothing plays. Focus, the status line, the Undo bar, and every sentence are unchanged, and no sentence is added. |

The click is made in the page, and the animations are caught and paused. Every `Element.prototype.animate()` call after the click is also recorded, and once the animations end each test checks that nothing started, in any later frame or task, beyond what was caught. The other is one `scale` from 1.12 to 1 at 420 ms on FAMILY's stack count, with a `linear()` easing. The rows show LEAD and FAMILY at once, and the cursor is on Undo. ## November 25 — A vote presses, the number rolls, and others' changes are marked on Update (B-01, B-02, B-06, M-02, M-15)

Build plan M3d-8. A new `web/src/board/vote-motion.ts` holds the motion. `web/src/board/Vote.tsx` presses the arrow and rolls the number, `web/src/board/session.ts` notes what each poll changed, `web/src/board/Board.tsx` and `web/src/board/Track.tsx` pass that to the votes, and Board rings the cards on Update. `web/src/app.css` gains the roll copy's base style. The service, `shared/`, `policy.ts`, the tokens, the prototype's CSS, every sentence, and the saved screenshots stay as they are.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| What was built | An arrow the reader may press plays `scale` 1, 0.92, 1 on the button at `--sm-fast` (120 ms). A blocked arrow (a Viewer, or a closed board) plays nothing. The number rolls at `--sm-medium` (200 ms) on `--sm-ease`. The real `.sm-vote-score` holds the new value and its `Net N` label from the first render, and it slides in from the far side, while an `aria-hidden` copy of the old figure, `.sm-vote-roll`, with no label, slides out in the vote's direction. The copy sits beside the number in the vote, not inside it, so the number's text stays one figure. Upright (importance), a higher number leaves the old one upward; on its side (articulation), whose "Better articulated" arrow points left, a higher number leaves it to the left. Each slide uses the `translate` property and a matching `clip-path` inset, so nothing is drawn outside the number's box, and nothing holds its end. The copy's base style is `opacity:0` and out of flow, so a finished or skipped animation never leaves it drawn, and it is removed when its animation finishes or is cancelled, never by a timer. A count rolls only for the reader's own press or for votes a poll brought: `session.ts` compares each poll's rows with the rows held before it and sets `recounted` to the arguments and wordings whose counts changed by votes, with the view that poll drew. Each vote rolls once for a given poll, in the render where that poll arrives, so a later change under the same view, such as a refused vote put back, changes at once. A second roll on the same number, from a quick second press or a poll, stops the one still running and removes its copy before it measures, and the copy takes the number's font by its longhand properties. A count that a combine, a separation, Undo, an edit, or Update changes changes at once, as it does when it is first drawn, so M3d-7's settle plays alone. No row moves on a poll (M-02). `session.ts` also keeps the wordings polls brought that are new or whose words changed, not deleted and not hidden, until the next Update. Update takes them, and once the shuffle has started, Board rings each one someone other than the reader wrote, by author, so the reader's own change from another tab isn't ringed. The ring goes on the wording's card where it is drawn, or else on its row's card, so a wording added to a folded row rings the row. It is the prototype's `flashIds`: a 3 px ring of `--sm-ink` at 35 percent that fades to nothing over 1.6 s, `ease-out`, with `composite: "add"`, so a card that moves on Update keeps its `--sm-lift` under the ring. The next Update stops any ring still fading before it measures. Votes alone ring nothing, since their counts already rolled when they arrived. Everything goes through `play()`, so under reduced motion nothing plays and no copy is made. No word appears near the arrow (B-06), the prototype's 1.4 s vote message stays unbuilt, and M2-11's sentences are unchanged. |

The service's answer and two polls start nothing more. It expects `boardMoves()`, every Web Animation on a row or anything in it, to be empty right after the poll that brings the other person's votes and the next poll. The row asks that count to roll on that poll, and the roll is a Web Animation on the number inside the row. Both polls run on the page's held clock and take about 30 ms of real time, so the 200 ms roll is still running: a trace shows only the number and its copy, on "Opening…". The test is protected (rule 9) and was not changed. The change it would need is for `boardMoves()` to leave out animations whose target is inside `.sm-vote` (for example `.filter((animation) => !target.closest(".sm-vote"))`), since a number rolling in its own box moves no row; or the builder drops the poll roll, or allows it as a CSS animation, which `boardMoves()` already leaves out. |
| Screenshots | Every saved screenshot in `screen`, `roles-on-screen`, `phone`, and `tour` still matches; none was replaced. Nothing rolls on mount or on a poll that changes nothing, and the copy is invisible at rest. |
| Products compared (rule 12) | Reddit's vote arrows, GitHub's reactions, and YouTube's like count, from memory, since a page fetched without a browser doesn't show their motion. Reddit fills the pressed arrow and changes the number, with no word. GitHub highlights the reaction pill and adds one to its count, with no word. YouTube rolls the like count's digits as the thumb animates. From Reddit and GitHub, only the pressed control and the new number show; from YouTube, the number rolls. The press and the ring's 3 px `--sm-ink` come from the prototype. No screen gains a label or a sentence, so rule 13 has nothing new to check. |
| Needs the builder | Decide the protected `shuffle-update.spec.ts` test above. The ring lasts 1.6 s, as the row and the prototype's `flashIds` say, from a constant in `vote-motion.ts`; the `--sm-ring` token (1.4 s) stays the link's ring, which `motion-system.spec.ts` holds. Say whether the Update ring should get a token of its own. The ring marks cards others added or reworded, not cards whose counts others' votes changed, since those already rolled on the poll, and not a source others cited, a moderator's hide, or someone else's combine. Say whether any of those should ring too. The architecture's §3 table still says a count updates in place "with a brief ring"; it is a plan file, so it was left as it is. M3d-8 isn't marked done. Watch a vote, a poll, and Update on staging at 1280 and 375 px. |

Its `boardMoves()` now leaves out animations inside a vote control, because a count rolling inside its own box moves no row. The test still fails if any row moves on a poll. |

## November 25 — Board and Strongest case zoom, and a board opens from its miniature (E-07, E-01, M-15)

Build plan M3d-9. A new `web/src/board/zoom.ts` holds the motion, ported from the prototype's `centerCard()`, `zoomTo()`, `growFrom()` and `shrinkInto()`. A new `web/src/opening.ts` holds what the in-page way between the list and a board keeps in memory. `web/src/App.tsx` changes the page inside itself for a board opened from the list and for Back to it, `web/src/Home.tsx` opens a board from a post and draws the list it holds on the way back, `web/src/board/Board.tsx` plays the zoom and the grow and handles Escape, `web/src/board/session.ts` says whether a board can be left quietly, `web/src/api.ts` notes each board a write reached, and `web/src/app.css` gains the copy's layer. The service, `shared/`, `policy.ts`, the tokens, the prototype's CSS, the Thumb, and the saved screenshots stay as they are.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| What was built | The View switch measures the card nearest the middle of the window, and the question's header, before React draws the other view. Once the new view is drawn, and once the cursor and a link's arrival have scrolled where they do today, the scene plays one animation from the transform that draws that card where it was to `none`, on `--sm-slow` and `--sm-ease`, with the transform origin only in the keyframes. Opening an argument from Strongest case zooms from that argument's card. A card the other view doesn't show falls back to the header, and nothing plays when nothing moved. Unlike the prototype, nothing scrolls, so each view ends where it is drawn without motion. A plain click on a post's question (the main button, no modifier key) opens its board inside the page with `history.pushState`, at the top of the page, and the board grows out of the rect the post's miniature had on the click: a translate and scale from that rect, and a clip that leaves the miniature's height, measured from the top, with its 6 px corners, to `none`, as `growFrom()` does. The board says "Opened “question”." as a link's board does. Back, or the wordmark on a board opened from the list, which then goes back in history as Back does, draws an inert, `aria-hidden` copy of the board where it was, with its textures, in a layer over the window. Home draws the list it already holds at once, with each board the reader wrote to since the list was read moved to the top, and scrolls it back to where it was left. The copy then shrinks into the post's miniature and fades to 0.6, as `shrinkInto()` does, and leaves the page when it ends, with a timer behind it. The cursor goes back to the post's question, and the list is read again. On a phone the list draws no miniature (the prototype's CSS hides it at 640 px and narrower), so the post itself is the rect. When the post isn't drawn or is off screen, the copy goes at once and the page changes with no motion, since the page transition is M3d-11's and isn't built. A reload, a deep link, and Forward play nothing, since nothing is kept outside memory. A board with actions waiting, or a held delete while offline, is left by loading the page, so the browser's leave prompt still asks (Q20): the wordmark then follows its link, and Back puts the board's address back and loads the list. Escape on Strongest case returns to Board with the cursor on the View switch, unless it is pressed in a menu, a panel, a dialog such as the share panel, a field, or on an open control. Everything goes through `play()`, so under reduced motion nothing plays and no copy is made. Whether a board can be left inside the page is asked of its session for as long as the board's page is open, so a board that a poll finds signed out, refused, or gone while actions wait still leaves by loading the page. Back with actions waiting puts back the address the board last gave its history entry, such as an open version or a linked comment, rather than the address it was opened at. Page views are counted as before, since analytics already count a change of address inside the page (`capture_pageview: "history_change"`), except that Back from a board with actions waiting counts the board once more when its address goes back. Nothing new is collected. |
| Checked by machine | `app/e2e/zoom-motion.spec.ts` has 22 browser tests, 10 at each of 1280 and 375 px and 2 at 1280 px, and all pass. After each caught animation, the tests let the page run 40 ms more and check that no other Web Animation is running, so "exactly one" below means one in all, not only one in the first batch. The View switch plays exactly one animation, on the scene, for 420 ms (`--sm-slow`), from a `scale()` to `none`. At its first frame the centred card is drawn in Strongest case within 1.5 px of where it was on the board. Choosing an argument in Strongest case other than the centred one plays exactly one animation, on the scene, and at its first frame that argument's card on the board is drawn within 1.5 px of where its card was in Strongest case. Each view ends at the places a page that switches under reduced motion draws, and at a fresh board's, within 1 px, with no style left on the scene. Opening from the list plays exactly one animation, on the surface, for 420 ms. Its first frame covers the miniature (or the post at 375 px) within 1.5 px, and its clip leaves the miniature's height within 1.5 px. It ends at a deep link's layout within 1 px, with no style left. Back plays exactly one animation, on the copy, which just before its end covers the miniature within 1.5 px. The copy then leaves the page, and the post's question has the cursor. After a vote, Back draws the voted board first and shrinks into its post at the top. With 13 boards and the voted board moved from the bottom to the top, Back restores the scroll, the post is off screen, no animation starts on the surface or its copy, and no copy is drawn. That test checks only that nothing shrinks: the page transition this case plays waits for M3d-11, whose row now checks it. A deep link, a reload, the wordmark from a deep link, Forward, and a reload of a board opened from the list start no Web Animation, counted from the page's load. Back, Forward, and the wordmark from a board opened from the list each land where expected. Escape on Strongest case returns to Board with the cursor on its button and says "Board.", and Escape on Board stays on the board. Escape in a field (the Acting as menu) on Strongest case keeps the cursor there and stays on Strongest case. Escape that closes the share panel in Strongest case leaves the view as it is, and the next Escape returns to Board. Under reduced motion the switch, opening, and Back start no Web Animation (`Element.prototype.animate` is recorded) and draw no copy. The board, Strongest case, and the list each pass an accessibility scan once the motion settles. Offline with a vote waiting, the wordmark and Back each bring up the browser's leave prompt, and dismissing it keeps the board, its address, and the waiting vote. Of the existing specs, untouched, `list`, `strongest-case`, `screen`, `phone`, `roles-on-screen`, `tour`, `accessibility`, `shuffle-update`, `hide-columns`, `unfold-motion`, `combine-motion`, `vote-motion`, `cards-arrive-leave`, `motion-system`, `leave-prompt`, `way-back`, `wires-texture`, `m3a-audit`, `options`, and `links` ran 210 tests: 208 pass, and the two in `shuffle-update.spec.ts` that already failed before this row (M3d-8's entry above) fail the same way. `comment-links`, `server-events`, `no-access`, `analytics`, `hints`, `sharing`, `history`, `closed-board`, `settings-design`, `new-steelman`, `offline-detection`, and `comments` ran 134 tests, and all pass. `web/test` (17 files, 119 tests) passes. `npm run typecheck` and ESLint on the changed files pass. The simulation was not run, by the lead's instruction on a loaded machine. |
| Tests first | All 18 browser tests were written first. 15 failed before the build: no zoom, grow, or shrink started, Escape did nothing on Strongest case, and the counted pages were loaded anew. 3 passed, which is right: the accessibility scans at both widths pass on today's views, and the leave prompt already asks when leaving a board loads the page. After the build, the two grow tests failed on the test's own reading of the clip, which the browser shortens; once that was fixed, the 375 px one showed a real bug: the board's height still changes while it grows on a phone, so a clip measured from the bottom hid the whole board at its first frame. The clip is now measured from the top, and all 18 pass. The off-screen test was renamed and now counts only animations on the surface or its copy, so that M3d-11 can add its transition without changing it. |
| Fails, protected | None from this row. The two `shuffle-update.spec.ts` failures at line 271 are M3d-8's, recorded in its entry, and are unchanged. |
| Screenshots | Every saved screenshot in `list`, `screen`, `roles-on-screen`, `phone`, and `tour` still matches; none was replaced. They come from deep links and loaded pages, which play nothing, and the Thumb and the markup are unchanged. |
| Products compared (rule 12) | Google Photos and Apple Photos, from memory: a photo opens by growing out of its thumbnail and closes by shrinking back into its place in the grid, which stays scrolled where it was, and the grid's focus returns to that thumbnail. From them, the grow and the shrink, the list kept as it was scrolled, and the cursor back on the post. iOS and macOS opening an app from its icon, from memory: the window grows from the icon's rect and shrinks back into it on close. Figma, Miro, and Google Maps, from memory: zooming scales about a focal point, so what is under it stays put; from them, the switch scales about the centred card. Apple's Human Interface Guidelines on motion and Material Design's motion system were tried live, but both pages render only by script and returned no text, so they weren't used. The only new sentence, "Opened “question”.", is the one a link's board already says, and it is a full sentence (rule 13). |

Until M3d-11, an off-screen post means no motion, and M3d-11's row now checks that its page transition plays there. Confirm that page views counted for a change of address inside the page are acceptable. A showing hint closes on the same Escape that returns to Board, rather than taking the first one. A board with actions waiting, left by Back, keeps an extra board entry in the history if the reader leaves. Watch the zoom, opening, and Back on staging at 1280 and 375 px. |

## November 25 — A polish pass on the board (A-01, A-02, M-14, M-15)

Build plan M3d-10. A new `web/src/board/Skeleton.tsx` draws the skeleton, and `web/src/board/Board.tsx` shows it while the first read is on its way. `web/src/board/drawer.ts` gains `useExit()`, the drawers' exit made general (`useDrawerExit()` now calls it), and `useLinger()`, which keeps a bar on screen while it fades. `web/src/board/Sharing.tsx`, `web/src/board/Card.tsx`, and `web/src/board/OptionMenu.tsx` use `useExit()`, and `Board.tsx`'s problem bar and Update pill use `useLinger()`. `web/src/app.css` holds the skeleton, the lift, the shared exit, and the focus ring, and `web/src/pages.css` moves the back button's ring. The service, the shared rules, the prototype, the tokens, and every saved screenshot are unchanged.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| What was built | First, while the board loads, skeleton cards stand in the board's own layout: the dark question with a bar for its words and its sharing pill, two dark option headers on their column panels, and placeholder cards with a vote stub, two lines, an author, and a footer. On a phone they show one column under the strip of sides, as the board does. They use their own classes, so no code that finds the board's cards, rows, or scene finds them. One `role="status"` sentence, "The steelman is loading.", says it to a screen reader. The status region is drawn empty and its sentence arrives just after, as Primer asks, so a screen reader hears a change to a region it already knows. The blocks are hidden from screen readers, and only they are marked `aria-busy`, so nothing holds the sentence back. The page keeps a `main` region and an unseen heading. The blocks wait twice `--sm-slow` (840 ms) before they fade in on `--sm-medium`, then pulse gently; under reduced motion they are drawn at once and stay still. Second, a card lifts 1 px under a hovering pointer, in `--sm-shadow-hover`, and settles to its resting shadow while its wording is pressed. The lift is the `translate` property, which adds to the board's moves on `transform` rather than replacing them, on `--sm-fast`. An open card and the cards of an unfolded row hold still, and a touch screen gets no hover. A card whose comments are open keeps its ink ring while it's pressed. Third, Share, Settings, History, the comments panel, the version menu, the option menu, the problem bar, and the Update pill share one entrance, the prototype's fade and 4 px rise on `--sm-medium`, and one exit, the same played back on `--sm-leave`. Share and the menus leave through an inert, `aria-hidden` copy, as the drawers already did. The copy has no IDs, field names, or keys left in it, and no role on itself, so nothing finds it twice. Everything inside it keeps its role and its actions, so the copy keeps the panel's look as it fades, such as a menu's red Delete and Share's narrow general-access row on a phone. The two bars stay one element and leave as themselves, keeping their centring, so every locator still finds one. Fourth, every control on the board, in Share, and in the panels and menus draws one focus ring: 2 px solid, in ink on a light ground and white on a dark one, 3 px out from a button, a link, or a choice, and 1 px out from a field. There are two exceptions, which are written down for a later row: a draft card's two fields keep the prototype's grey fill with no ring (finding 10), and the New steelman fields keep their ring 3 px out (finding 7). The app.css value that duplicated a token, the linked comment's `1400ms`, is now `var(--sm-ring)`. |
| The audit, against the prototype (`docs/prototype/source/steelman-refined.html`) and the brief (`docs/design/README.md`) | Each finding with its before and after follows. **Loading.** Before: the board's area was empty, with no status, until the first read arrived. After: the skeleton and its status sentence, as above. **Card hover.** Before: the prototype's hover darkened a card's border and deepened its shadow, with no lift, though the brief's Hover row asks for "shadow, and lift". After: the 1 px lift and the press settle; the border tint stays. **Panels closing.** Before: only the three drawers faded out; Share, both menus, the problem bar, and the pill vanished at once, as the prototype and the brief's Panels row ("They close instantly.") have it. After: all eight share one exit. **A closing drawer's copy.** Before: M3d-4's rule for a deleted card's copy (`.sm-leaving`, `position:absolute` and a dashed edge) also matched a closing drawer's copy, so the drawer's copy jumped 56 px from where the drawer was, with a dashed edge, as it faded. After: that rule matches only the copies in the leaving layer. **Focus rings.** Before: links (the wordmark, New steelman, your account, buttons drawn as links), every select (Share's roles and access, the test-user switch), and the date and time fields in Settings drew the browser's 1 px default ring. Share's controls drew a white ring on a white panel, since Share hangs inside the dark question. An option header's buttons, the phone's sides, the Update pill's button, the problem bar's Dismiss, and the analytics notice's buttons drew an ink ring on ink. Share's invite field showed only a darker edge. The Report note's field had no ring. An option's name field and a choice sat 2 px out, a list post 2 px out, and the back button 1 px out. After: all of them draw the one ring. **Tokens.** Before: `1400ms` written out in app.css. After: `var(--sm-ring)`. No other app.css value equals a token, and `docs/design/tokens.css` has no spacing tokens, so nothing else was swapped. |
| Found, for a later row | These were written down, not built. (1) The brief's Panels row and the prototype's comment still say panels "close instantly", which the app no longer does; the builder decides the wording. (2) Near-duplicates of tokens, which a swap would change by a few pixels: `.sm-hint`'s shadow `0 8px 18px rgb(15 23 42 / 20%)`, `.sm-problem`'s `0 6px 18px rgb(0 0 0 / .18)`, `#b4323f` beside `--sm-alert` `#B4232F`, the `#c8c8c8` field borders, the previous-wording panel's `#e4e0d6`, `#f3f1ec`, and `#a8a39a`, and the privacy page's `#3a3a3a`. A spacing scale would need new tokens. (3) The skeleton matches a Contributor's board; an Owner's or Editor's board also has the column to add an option. (4) If the first read can't reach the service, the skeleton stays with no offline note, since the pill lives with the board, and `readBoard()` throws a plain error on an unexpected status. (5) Share's people list says "Loading…", a fragment under rule 13. (6) On a phone, the header hides the wordmark's word and the View switch until the board shows, as before. (7) The New steelman question and options keep the prototype's ring 3 px out around a field, which the saved `new-steelman` screenshots show. (8) The AI glyph's ochre ring waits for the AI rows. (9) A lifted card's wire end stays at the card's resting place, 1 px off, until the pointer leaves. (10) A draft card's two fields (New argument, Suggest a version, and the Owner's Edit form) draw no focus ring. Before and after: the prototype's rule `.sm-compose-card textarea:focus-visible { outline:none; background:#f6f6f6; }` shows focus as a grey fill, because the card itself is the field. Giving them the one ring would change the saved card-form screenshot, so the builder decides. (11) The Update pill's status says "Offline · 1 waiting to send", "Back online · 1 sent", and "3 changes", and the problem bar's offline line ends in "· 1 waiting to send". These are fragments joined by middle dots, which rule 13 rules out. Rewording them, for example to "You're offline, and 1 change is waiting to send.", would change existing tests, so the builder decides. |

Only the hidden blocks are `aria-busy`, and the page has one `main`, one heading, nothing the board's code looks for, two columns at 1280 px and one at 375 px with at least two cards each, full opacity once its fade-in ends, and no sideways scroll; released, the board replaces it, and the skeleton's question and columns stood within 2 px of the board's in left and width. ## November 25 — A polish pass on Home, New steelman, Your account, and the privacy page (E-03, L-04, M-13, M-15)

Build plan M3d-11. A new `web/src/paging.ts` lets the browser's view transition play only between Home, New steelman, Your account, and the privacy page, and `web/index.html` opts the pages in to it. `web/src/Home.tsx` draws skeleton posts while the list loads and raises the list when Back from a board has no post on screen to shrink into, and `web/src/board/zoom.ts` fades the board's copy in that case. A new `web/src/busy.ts` gives the buttons that wait on Steelman one busy state: Create steelman, Download, Sign out, Delete account, the email-change steps' Send code, Change email, and Send a new code, the sign-in steps' Email me a code, Sign in, and Send a new code, Choose a username's Continue, and the no-access card's Sign in with another account. A new `web/src/rows.ts` raises Your account's rows. `web/src/Account.tsx` and `web/src/signIn/ChangeEmail.tsx` carry the rewritten lines, `web/src/NewSteelman.tsx` leaves out its sheet's own entrance when the page arrives through the transition, and `web/src/app.css` holds the transition, the skeleton, the post's lift, the press, and the busy look. `web/src/board/motion.ts` now exports its stagger. The service, the shared rules, the prototype, the tokens, and every saved screenshot are unchanged.

## November 25 — Production at steelman-argument.com, drafted (M-06, M-08)

Build plan M3d-12, drafted. `infra/production/main.tf` sets `domain = "steelman-argument.com"`, and its first line names the new address. `infra/README.md` gives the new address in its table, says in step 7 that a bare address takes `A` and `AAAA` records named `@` instead of a `CNAME`, and ends with a new section, "Moving production to steelman-argument.com", which holds the builder's steps in the row's order, each command in its own block. `SECURITY.md` line 9 says that M3d-12 moves production and that the old address then forwards there. No app code changed, because the sign-in base URL, the trusted origin, and the Origin check all follow `APP_ORIGIN`. The test helper `server/test/support/sign-in.ts` takes an optional `origin`, which sets the service's origin, the Origin a request sends, and Google's return address together. Its default is still `https://steelman.test`, so every existing caller behaves as before. Nothing ran against production, and the builder's run is still to come, so M3d-12 isn't marked done.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| What was built | The service started with its origin set to `https://steelman-argument.com`, which is the value `main.ts` passes on from `APP_ORIGIN`, accepts a vote sent from that address. It refuses a vote sent from `https://app.steelman-argument.com` with 403 `wrong_origin`. It sends Google `https://steelman-argument.com/api/auth/callback/google` as the return address, and after Google it sends the browser back to the page on the new address that asked. The browser and the service both tag events from `steelman-argument.com` and `www.steelman-argument.com` as production, and still tag `staging.steelman-argument.com` as staging. The test can't import `main.ts` itself, because `main.ts` reads the environment and starts listening when it loads, so it hands the origin to the service the way `main.ts` does. |
| Checked by machine | `web/test/apex-host.test.ts` (3), `server/test/apex-host.test.ts` (3), and `server/test/api/apex-origin.test.ts` (3) pass. The untouched `web/test/analytics.test.ts` (5) still passes. Every file that uses the sign-in helper still passes: `sign-in` (27), `google-sign-in` (34), `change-email` (11), `new-board` (21), `roles` (73), `sign-in-fields` (11), `sign-in-limit` (5), `sign-in-long-input` (5), and `sign-in-network-limit` (7). So do `server-events` (6), `smoke` (3), `deploy-trust` (6), which reads `infra/production/main.tf`, `protected-files` (5), and `feature-tests-built` (3), 231 tests in all. `npm run typecheck` and eslint on the changed files pass. The protected-files guard says no protected files changed. `terraform fmt -check -recursive infra` passes. `terraform validate` passes on a scratch copy of `infra/production` and the module, made with `init -backend=false` from the providers already downloaded, with no state and no `terraform.tfvars`. |
| Tests first | All nine tests were written first. To show the unit tests catch a mistake, both copies of `environmentOf` (`web/src/analytics.ts` and `server/src/events.ts`) were broken for one run so that only a name starting `app.` counted as production. Four unit tests failed then, because the bare address and `www` came out as staging. The two staging tests passed, which is right, because the break leaves staging as staging. Both files were then copied back from a saved copy, and `git diff --exit-code` on `web/src` and `server/src` showed no change. In the same run, before the helper took an origin, two API tests failed: the vote from the new address got 403 `wrong_origin`, and Google's sign-in was refused, because the service still believed it was `https://steelman.test`. The test that the old address is refused passed, which is right, because the old address is refused before and after. After the restore and the helper's option, all nine pass. |
| Expected plan | This is read from the code, not from a run. `terraform plan` should say `Plan: 2 to add, 2 to change, 2 to destroy.` It replaces `google_cloud_run_domain_mapping.app`, because its name changes, and `google_monitoring_uptime_check_config.health`, because its host changes. It updates `google_cloud_run_v2_service.app` in place, because `APP_ORIGIN` changes, which makes a new revision on the image already running. It updates `google_monitoring_alert_policy.down` in place, because its filter names the new check. That the uptime check is replaced, rather than updated, comes from memory of the Google provider. That the new revision takes the traffic is inferred from the last `update-traffic --to-latest` and wasn't checked. |
| Risk for the builder | The apply may stop with a 409 on the service, as `terraform apply` did on November 23 ("PostHog on the live app"), because Terraform sends the revision name the last deploy gave the service. Production could then be down at both addresses until the run finishes. The README gives a proposed way through, which hasn't been run: set `APP_ORIGIN` with `gcloud run services update`, as on November 23, then plan and apply again. The apply may also stop at the uptime check, because Terraform deletes the old check before it makes the new one while the alert policy still names it, and Cloud Monitoring may refuse that. That refusal comes from memory and wasn't checked. The README says to stop there and bring the output to an issue. A lasting fix, `create_before_destroy` on the uptime check in `infra/modules/steelman/main.tf`, is outside the row, so the module isn't changed, and the builder decides on the pull request whether to add it before the run. The smoke test doesn't check `APP_ORIGIN`, so step 9 also has the builder read the service's settings with `gcloud run services describe` and then sign in and make one write, which must pass. The README also says to stop before applying if the plan shows any other change, such as the PostHog key that was set outside Terraform on November 23. |
| Effects on people (question 2) | These come from reading the code, not from research with people. Everyone signs in once more, because the sign-in cookie belongs to the one address that set it. The browser keeps the analytics choice for each address separately, so at the new address the notice asks again, and PostHog loads for someone who turned analytics off at `app.steelman-argument.com` until they press Turn off again. Global Privacy Control and Do Not Track still turn analytics off there. Hidden columns on a board are kept for each address too, so they show again. Sentences the privacy page could carry, which haven't been added, are: "Steelman is now at steelman-argument.com instead of app.steelman-argument.com. You sign in once more at the new address. Your analytics choice stays with the old address, so analytics are on at the new address until you turn them off again. Global Privacy Control and Do Not Track still turn them off." |
| Needs the builder | The builder runs the steps in `infra/README.md`, "Moving production to steelman-argument.com": the Google OAuth client's new redirect URI, the production Terraform plan and apply, the Cloudflare records and redirects, the checks, and removing the old redirect URI. The builder also decides whether to accept the two effects on people on the pull request or to have the privacy page say them. After the run, a dated entry records the plan's output and each check, and M3d-12 is marked done. |
| Not checked | Nothing ran against production, Cloudflare, or the OAuth client. The plan wasn't run. The Cloudflare `www` record and the redirect rule's wording come from memory of Cloudflare's guidance. The Product Page's link to the app, in `docs/index.html`, waits for a follow-up after the move. |

## November 25 — Search and sort your steelmen, with comment counts (E-03, E-04, E-05)

Build plan M3d-13. `server/src/list.ts` gives each listing two more fields read from data already kept: `createdAt`, the board's creation time, and `comments`, its open comment count. `shared/src/api.ts` adds both to `BoardListing`. A new `web/src/listSearch.ts` holds the search match, the three sorts, their sentences, and the tab's saved sort. `web/src/Home.tsx` draws the search field, the Sort menu, each post's comment count, the no-match box, and the motion, and its skeleton gains the toolbar and a count bar. `web/src/icons.tsx` gains Lucide's `search`, and `web/src/app.css` holds the toolbar, the count, the fading copies, and the no-match box. No migration, policy, write, or request input changed, and nothing new is collected about people.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| What was built | Home has a "Search steelmen" field and a Sort button above the list. Typing filters the posts at once by the question and by each option's name, whatever the case. The filter runs in the browser over the list the page already holds, so the search text never reaches the service or PostHog. The field carries `ph-no-capture`, nothing on the page repeats the text, and it's kept in the page's memory only, so it's still there after Back but never stored. With no match, a dashed box says "No steelmen match that search." and offers Clear search, which empties the field and puts the cursor back in it. The Sort menu offers Recently updated (the order the service already gives), Newest (the board made last first), and Needs help (each board with an option under two arguments first, then the rest, both in the same order). Each choice has a sentence under it, and the chosen one has a check. The Sort button shows the chosen sort after the word Sort, as GitHub's does, so a reader who comes back to Newest sees why the order changed, and a screen reader hears "Sort Newest". At 480 px and narrower the word Sort isn't drawn but is still said, so the field keeps room for its placeholder, and the skeleton's Sort block is as wide as the button with its default. The sort is kept in the tab's sessionStorage, so it survives Back (in-page and by page load) and a reload, and the address stays `/`. A screen reader hears "2 steelmen match that search." or the chosen sort's sentence. Each post shows its open comment count with the speech bubble, outside its options and above the post's covering link, so its tooltip ("1 comment") shows. Posts a filter or a sort keeps slide to their new places with `flip()`, and posts it brings back fade in. Posts it drops fade out from where they stood, as inert copies in a layer under the posts that carry no link, ID, or ARIA attribute, and the copies are removed when the fade ends. Under reduced motion nothing moves and no copy is made. The first drawing, Back, and a fresh read of the list move nothing. |
| The count's rule | The count copies the board's speech bubbles (`openCount` in `shared/src/comments.ts`) over the comments the board sends a reader (`commentRows` in `server/src/read.ts`). Replies count. Deleted comments, every comment in a resolved thread, and comments on a deleted or hidden wording or a deleted option don't count, and nor do other boards' comments. A deleted first comment's live replies still count. A comment a moderator hid still counts, because the board counts it (`shared/test/comments.test.ts`, "a hidden comment counts, as the prototype counts it"), so the count is the same for every reader. The row says the count "leaves out deleted and hidden comments" and also that it equals the board's own count; these conflict for a hidden comment, so "hidden" was read as a hidden wording, and the count equals the board's. |
| Rule 13 | Every new line is a sentence or a short label. Labels: "Search steelmen", "Sort", "Recently updated", "Newest", "Needs help", and "Clear search". The Sort button reads as "Sort" and the chosen sort, such as "Sort Newest". Sentences: "The steelmen that changed last come first.", "The newest steelmen come first.", "Steelmen with an option under two arguments come first.", "No steelmen match that search.", "1 steelman matches that search.", and "3 steelmen match that search.". The prototype's announcement "Sorted by new." was a fragment and isn't used. The count's name is "1 comment" or "3 comments", as the option counts' "3 arguments". |
| Checked by machine | `app/server/test/api/list-comment-counts.test.ts` (4), `app/web/test/list-search.test.ts` (12), and `app/e2e/list-search.spec.ts` (13: 6 at each of 1280 and 375 px, and the PostHog check) pass. ## November 25 — Your statistics, shown only to you (L-09, M-07, L-04)

Build plan M3d-14. A new `server/src/statistics.ts` works out the counts, and `server/src/app.ts` answers them at `GET /api/account/statistics` beside the other account reads, with no rate limit, since reading isn't counted (architecture §7). `shared/src/api.ts` adds the `AccountStatistics` shape, and `shared/src/strongest.ts` exports its ranking as `rankLive`, which Strongest case and the statistics now share. A new `web/src/statistics.ts` turns the counts into sentences, `web/src/Account.tsx` draws the group, `web/src/rows.ts` gives its lines a stagger of their own, `web/src/pages.css` styles it, and `web/src/Privacy.tsx` gains the row's sentence and the date November 25. No migration, table, policy, write, or request input changed, nothing is stored, and nothing new is collected about people.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| What was built | Your account has a "Your statistics" group after the Username card and before Tour, inside `ph-no-capture`. Under its heading, a sentence says "Only you can see these numbers." Each line is one sentence with an icon, such as "You wrote 1 argument, and it is in its option’s top three.", "You wrote 2 wordings, and 1 of them leads its row.", "You haven’t added a source yet.", "You haven’t written a comment yet.", and "You took part in 1 board." A zero line is drawn in the quiet colour, as the prototype's profile draws zero points. A new account sees one line, "You haven’t written anything yet." The statistics are read together with the account, so the page is drawn once, at its full height. If they can't be read, the group says "Steelman couldn’t count what you wrote. Reload the page to try again." The lines rise into place on their own stagger, starting one turn after Username, so the other rows keep their turns, and nothing moves under reduced motion. The privacy page's Who sees what list adds "Steelman shows you counts of what you wrote, worked out from what it already keeps, and no one else sees them." |
| What counts | Only boards where the person has a `board_member` role on a board that isn't deleted, as the list reads them; a space will add a second way in (M3d-17). Arguments count when they were written by the person (`argument.created_by`), aren't deleted or merged, stand on an option that isn't deleted, and still have a wording shown. Wordings count when they were written by the person and are neither deleted nor hidden, on such an argument; a wording combined into another argument still counts. Sources count when the person added them and a wording that's shown cites them now (`revision_source` on the wording's current revision), on an argument that isn't deleted or merged and an option that isn't deleted, since a board shows a source only under a wording. Comments and replies count when they are neither deleted nor hidden and their wording or option is still there; resolved threads count. "In its option’s top three" and "leads its row" follow Strongest case (`rankLive` and `CASE_SIZE`) over the newest published order, then what it hasn't ranked yet in the order added; reading the statistics never publishes an order. A board counts as one the person took part in only when they wrote something counted there, so joining by the link doesn't count. No vote, assessment, combine, or report table is read. |
| Rule 13 | The heading "Your statistics" is a label. Every other line is a full present- or past-tense sentence, and one, many, and none each have their own wording: "it is" or "it isn’t" for one, "1 of them is" or "3 of them are" for some, and "none of them are" for none. The sentences use the curly apostrophe, as the rest of Your account does. |
| Checked by machine | `app/server/test/api/statistics.test.ts` (7), `app/shared/test/rank-live.test.ts` (3), `app/web/test/statistics.test.ts` (5), and `app/e2e/statistics.spec.ts` (7: two at each of 1280 and 375 px, one for writing, one for a failed read, and "L-09, M-07: the privacy page says only you see your statistics") pass. The API tests check every count on a seeded board, and that the top three and lead wordings change once others' votes publish a new order. They check that deleted, hidden, and merged items, comments on a hidden wording or a deleted option, a source whose only citing wording is hidden or deleted, a board the person was removed from, a deleted board, and a board whose link was turned off are left out. They check that Ana's, Ben's, and a voter's numbers are each their own and that votes and assessments, theirs or anyone's, change nothing. They also check that a new account gets zeros even after opening a board by its link, and that signed out or deleted gets 401 `{status: "refused", reason: "signed_out"}`. One test hides the only wording of an argument by someone else, ranked above the person's, and checks that it takes no place, so the person's argument moves into the top three; hiding the only wording of the person's own argument then takes away the argument, its wording, and the source only it cites. Removing either hidden-wording filter, or the cited-source filter, from `statistics.ts` makes that test fail; this was seen. The browser tests check the group's place after Username, its `ph-no-capture`, its note, its sentences, that a comment written through the API shows next time while someone else's numbers stay theirs, a new account's one sentence, that the lines fit the card, and the accessibility scan. When the statistics read answers 500, the group says "Steelman couldn’t count what you wrote. Reload the page to try again." with no list, the Username row and the Privacy group still show, and the scan passes. Signed out, the privacy page shows the new sentence and "Updated November 25, 2026". Unchanged files pass: the web, shared, and account API unit files (36 files, 292 tests, including `strongest-case.test.ts`; 39 files and 307 tests with the three new ones), the feature-test and boundary guards (27), and the browser specs `account`, `account-card`, `privacy`, `privacy-history`, `settings-design`, `pages-polish`, `accessibility`, `sign-out`, `phone`, `sign-in-methods`, `change-email`, `tour`, `way-back`, and `strongest-case`. `npm run typecheck` and eslint on the changed files pass. |
| Tests first | The API, shared, and web unit tests were written first and seen failing: the 6 API tests got 404 (no route), the 3 shared tests found no `rankLive`, and the web file found no `statistics.ts`. One API test also had my own setup slip, a reply sent without its wording, which the service refused as `not_same_thread`. The browser spec was written before the screen but first run after it, so it wasn't seen failing. After the build, its sideways-scroll check failed for a signed-in account at 375 px because the Email row is 377 px wide with the group removed too, so the check now measures the group itself. |

Stack Overflow's profile stats (reputation, answers, questions, and reached) are from memory, since the help page couldn't be fetched; from them, each count is shown with its zeros. The icons and the quieter zero lines come from the prototype's profile Points. |

## November 25 — Change your username (L-01, L-04, M-01, M-07)

Build plan M3d-15. A new migration, `server/migrations/0015_username_hold.sql`, adds the `username_hold` table (`old_name`, `account_id`, `ends_at`) with a unique index on `lower(old_name)`, an index on the account, and `select`, `insert`, and `delete` for the service, and no length or value checks (Q24). A new `server/src/username.ts` holds the change, the claim at first sign-in, the daily release, and the export's hold. `server/src/app.ts` answers `PUT /api/account/username`, `server/src/sign-in.ts` adds a before-hook on Better Auth's `/update-user`, `server/src/jobs/daily.ts` deletes ended holds, and `server/src/account.ts` lists the hold in Download your data. `shared/src/api.ts` adds `usernameChangeInput` and `UsernameChangeAnswer`. On the web, a new `web/src/signIn/ChangeUsername.tsx` draws the Username row and its field, a new `web/src/signIn/usernameRules.ts` holds the sentences Choose a username already said (its text is unchanged), `web/src/when.ts` adds `monthDay`, `web/src/api.ts` adds `changeMyUsername`, and `web/src/Privacy.tsx` gains the row's sentence. The page's date stays November 25. `SECURITY.md` notes that the change has no rate limit.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| What was built | The Username row on Your account has Change beside the name; its accessible name is "Change username". Change opens a field labelled "New username" that holds the current name, selected, with the hint "Your old username stays reserved for you for 30 days, and links to it lead to your new one." and Cancel and Save. Enter saves and Escape cancels, and the cursor goes back to Change. A second Enter while a save is waiting sends nothing, and an answer that comes back after Cancel or Escape leaves the form alone, though a name that did change still shows on the row. Success says "Your username is now omar_hassan." in the page's status line, and Delete account then asks for the new name. A wrong shape says "Use 3 to 20 letters, numbers, or underscores.", a reserved name says "admin is reserved.", a taken or held name says "jordan_w is taken.", your own name says "That’s already your username.", a second change says "You can change your username again on Dec 16.", and a failure says "Steelman couldn’t change your username. Try again." Bylines, History, comments, and the people list read the name from the account, so they show the new name with no other change. |
| The rules | Both ways of claiming a name run in one transaction that first takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(lower(name)))`, then checks `account` and `username_hold` in one statement, so a claim of a name someone is leaving sees either their account or their new hold. The hook at first sign-in writes the name itself under that lock, and Better Auth then writes the same name, so a hold can't land between a check and a write. It refuses a held name with the username plugin's own code, `USERNAME_IS_ALREADY_TAKEN`, so the sign-in page says "<name> is taken.". A change holds the old name until 30 days later. While a person's hold lasts, a second change answers 409 `too_soon` with the hold's end, and the browser says the day in the reader's time zone. Taking your own held name back ends its hold, holds nothing for the name you leave, and uses up no change, so one later change is still allowed. A hold keeps the name from everyone else until the daily job deletes it, even after its end has passed, as a deleted account's name stays taken until that job releases it; the job adds the count to its existing `usernames` total. Its owner can still take the name back in that time. Sending your current name answers 200 `already_done` and changes nothing. An invitation by a held old name answers 404, so Share says "No one is called lin_old. Check the username." with no change to `sharing.ts`. Deleting the account leaves the hold alone until it ends. A deleted account's own name is still held by `releaseHeldUsernames`. |
| Rule 13 | "Change", "Cancel", "Save", and "New username" are labels. The hint, every refusal, the success line, and the privacy sentence are full sentences, with the curly apostrophe. The row's example names, lin.zhao, have a dot, which the username rule refuses, so the tests use names such as lin_zhao. |
| Checked by machine | New files pass: `app/server/test/api/username-change.test.ts` (16), `app/server/test/database/username-hold.test.ts` (5), `app/web/test/username-change.test.ts` (2), and `app/e2e/username-change.spec.ts` (7: three at each of 1280 and 375 px, and one at 1280 px for a save that waits). The API tests cover a change and the new name in the snapshot, History, and comments; the old name refused to someone else for 30 days by a change and by first sign-in, still refused after the hold's end until the daily job deletes it, then free; its owner taking an ended hold's name back before the job runs; two changes, and a change and a first sign-in, sent while the test holds the name's lock, leaving exactly one owner (the test lets the lock go only once both claims wait on that name's key in its own database); taking a name back; taken, invalid, and reserved names; the second change and its date; the same name twice; the invitation; `/api/people/<old name>`; signed out (401), another origin (403 `wrong_origin`), and an account with no name yet (403 `needs_username`); the export; and deletion. The database tests cover the unique index in other capitals, the foreign key, the not-null columns, the service's grants (it can't update a hold), and the migration as an upgrade over a database with accounts. The browser tests cover Change, Save, the success sentence, Cancel, Escape, the keyboard alone, a second Enter while a save waits, an answer that comes back after Escape, each refusal with `aria-invalid` and the sentence read out, the new name after a reload and on a board's byline, no sideways scroll, and the accessibility scan with the field open. Unchanged files pass: `sign-in.test.ts`, `sign-in-fields.test.ts`, `account.test.ts`, `daily.test.ts`, `invites.test.ts`, `service-login.test.ts`, `migrations.test.ts`, `change-email.test.ts`, `statistics.test.ts`, `google-sign-in.test.ts`, `test-accounts.test.ts`, `identity-and-deletion.test.ts`, `sign-in-tables.test.ts`, `people.test.ts`, `test-user.test.ts`, `bad-requests.test.ts`, `security-headers.test.ts`, all 21 web unit files (141 tests), and the browser specs `account`, `sign-in`, `invite`, `statistics`, `privacy`, `change-email`, `sign-out`, `pages-polish`, `accessibility`, `phone`, and `account-card`. `npm run typecheck` and eslint on the changed files pass. |

Two of my own setup slips showed up on the first run after the build: the file's IDs used the same seed as the service's, and the test clock can't move backwards. ## November 26 — Profiles, with bylines that link to them (L-03, L-04, M-07, M-08)

Build plan M3d-16, not marked done: the privacy wording waits for the builder, and two protected tests need the builder's change. A new `server/src/profiles.ts` reads a profile in one repeatable-read transaction, and `server/src/app.ts` answers `GET /api/people/:username`. `shared/src/api.ts` adds `Profile` and `ProfileItem`. No migration is needed, and `policy.ts` and `access.ts` are unchanged. On the web, `web/src/links.ts` adds `/u/<username>`, `profilePath`, and `hasProfile`; a new `web/src/Profile.tsx` draws the page; a new `web/src/UserLink.tsx` makes a name a link; `web/src/board/Card.tsx` (bylines), `web/src/board/Sharing.tsx` (the people list), and `web/src/board/History.tsx` (each entry's name) use it; `web/src/App.tsx` routes the page; `web/src/paging.ts` gives it the page transition; `web/src/analytics.ts` adds a `before_send` that rewrites profile addresses; `web/src/api.ts` adds `readProfile`; `web/src/Account.tsx` gives Your statistics the anchor `#statistics`; `web/src/app.css` and `web/src/pages.css` style the links and the page; and `web/src/Privacy.tsx` gains the row's sentence. The browser tests' own file server, `e2e/support/board.ts`, now serves the page for `/u/lin.zhao`, whose ending looked like a file's extension.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| What was built | `/u/<username>` shows the way back, a dark header with the person's initials and username, with "you" after your own name and a "Your statistics" link to `/account#statistics`, then a Contributions card. Its tabs read "Arguments 2", "Wordings 1", and "Sources 1"; they follow the ARIA tab pattern, so Tab reaches only the chosen tab and the arrow keys move between them. Each item is a link to its card (`/b/<board>/v/<version>`), with the wording or the source's name, the board's question, and "Added 3d ago", whose tooltip gives the full date. An argument shows and opens the earliest shown wording its creator wrote, so a profile never shows another person's words as someone's argument. When the creator's first wording is hidden while someone else's stays shown, or an older wording by someone else is combined in, the argument still shows its creator's own words, and an argument with no shown wording by its creator isn't listed. A source opens the earliest shown wording that cites it now. As in the prototype's tabs, the wording that started a person's own argument is that argument, so Wordings holds the rest and nothing is counted twice. Items are newest first, at most 500 per tab. A name held after a change answers with the new name, and `history.replaceState` moves the address to it. An unknown name shows "Profile not found" with "No one is called x. Check the username." and a link to Your steelmen. Signed out, the page asks for sign-in and reads nothing. Bylines, the people list, and History's names are links to profiles inside `ph-no-capture`, in the text's own colour, with an underline only on hover; `deleted_account`, an empty name, and "No username yet" stay plain text. Comment authors and Home's owner name aren't links, because the row doesn't name them. |
| Who sees what | The viewer's boards are their own `board_member` rows with a known role, on boards that aren't deleted, as Your statistics reads them, with a note where M3d-18's space role goes. Nothing calls `entryTo` or `joinByLink`, so a board open to anyone with the link counts only after the viewer has opened it, and reading a profile records no membership. Removing the viewer, or turning the link off, drops that board at once. The question is read only through the join on those boards. Hidden and deleted wordings, deleted and merged arguments, arguments on a deleted option, and sources no shown wording cites are left out, even for the board's Owner. No vote, assessment, or comment table is read, and the answer has only `username`, `you`, `now`, and the three lists. A name is looked up in `account` first, then in `username_hold` while its hold lasts (by the service's clock) and its account isn't deleted; a hold that has ended, a deleted account's name and its hold, `deleted_account`, and a name of the wrong shape answer 404 `{reason: "not_found"}`. A viewer whose account is deleted gets 401. The route has no rate limit and sends no server event, because reading isn't counted, and the request log names only the route. |
| No name reaches PostHog | PostHog sends the page's address with every event, the page before it, and a clicked link's address. `withoutProfileNames` rewrites every `/u/<name>` in an event, wherever it sits, to `/u/:username`, and leaves anything that isn't a string, an array, or a plain object as it is. The page is inside `ph-no-capture`, and someone else's profile is named "the profile" in the way back from Your account, so no name sits in that link's text. That way back still has the profile's address in its `href`, and PostHog compresses replay snapshots before `before_send` could see them, so `BackLink.tsx` puts a way back to any `/u/` address inside `ph-no-capture`, which replays skip. |
| Rule 13 | "Arguments", "Wordings", "Sources", "Contributions", "Your statistics", and "Profile not found" are labels. "There is nothing here that you can open.", which your own profile says too because what you wrote on a board you've left or that is hidden is left out, "There are no arguments here yet.", "There are no other wordings here yet.", "There are no sources here yet.", "No one is called x. Check the username.", "Steelman couldn’t read this profile. Reload the page to try again.", and the privacy sentence are full sentences. Each item's line is the board's question, a dot, and "Added 3d ago", which follows Home's "Updated 3d ago". |

One color-contrast failure came from a scan taken while a just-chosen tab's colour was still fading, so the scan now waits for running animations to finish, and the spec then passed four times over (40 of 40). The API tests cover the items with their board, question, card, and age; your own profile; an invited board's items and question never appearing; a link board left out until the viewer opens it, and reading twice changing nothing; a person who shares no board; removal and turning the link off; hidden and deleted items for the Owner; an argument whose creator's first wording is hidden while another person's stays shown, and an argument with an older wording combined in from someone else, each showing only its creator's words; items on a deleted option and on a deleted board, the board deleted alone and with its last owner's account; newest first in each tab; the exact keys, with votes and an assessment saved; 404 for an unknown name, a deleted account's name, `deleted_account`, and a wrong shape; a held name answering with the new one, an ended hold and a deleted account's hold answering 404; and 401 signed out and for a deleted viewer.
