## November 26 — Spaces on the service (G-03, G-04, M-07, M-12)

Build plan M3d-17, not marked done: the new tables in the schema list, the space actions in `policy.ts`, and the privacy sentence wait for the builder. A new migration, `server/migrations/0016_spaces.sql`, adds `space`, `space_member`, and a third table, `space_change`, with no length or value checks (Q24) and no `board.space_id` (M3d-18). `server/src/db/database.ts` gains their types. `shared/src/policy.ts` adds, below the board table and without changing it, `SpaceRole`, `SPACE_ROLES`, `SpaceAction`, `SPACE_POLICY`, and `canInSpace()`. `shared/src/inputs.ts` adds `SPACE_LIMITS` (40 and 120) apart from `LIMITS`. `shared/src/api.ts` adds the space inputs, the answer types, and the refusal `owner_keeps_role`. A new `server/src/spaces.ts` holds the space write path and the reads, and `server/src/app.ts` answers the routes under `/api/spaces`. `server/src/account.ts` adds the export's `spaces` and the hand-over on account deletion, and `web/src/Privacy.tsx` gains the row's sentence. No screen is built (M3d-19).

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| What was built | `POST /api/spaces` takes a browser-made ID, a name, and an optional about text, canvas, and visibility. Any signed-in account may make a space and becomes its one Owner. A new space starts private on the stone canvas. `GET /api/spaces` lists only your own spaces by name, `GET /api/spaces/:space` reads one, and `GET /api/spaces/:space/people` lists its members. Your spaces and a single space each give the reader's role and the member count, and the member list gives each member's username and role. `PUT /api/spaces/:space` sets the name, about text, and canvas. `PUT /api/spaces/:space/visibility` takes only "private". `POST /api/spaces/:space/invites` adds someone by username as Admin or Member. `PUT /api/spaces/:space/people/:account` switches a role between Admin and Member. `DELETE /api/spaces/:space/people/:account` removes a member, and when someone names themself, they leave. Each read asks `canInSpace()` for `space_read` with the reader's role, so the policy table decides who reads. Each write locks the space's row, reads the person's own role, asks `canInSpace()`, loads what it refers to, and makes the change with a `space_change` row, all in one transaction. A refused or already-done write is rolled back. The Owner can't be demoted or removed and can't leave, and those requests answer 409 `owner_keeps_role`. Inviting the Owner gets the same answer. Inviting someone already in the space at their own role answers `already_done`, and at the other role it changes their role, as a board invite does. An invite to an unknown or deleted username is not found. A role change for someone not in the space, or for an account that doesn't exist, is not found. Removing someone not in the space answers `already_done`, and removing an account that doesn't exist is not found. Making a space with the same ID twice makes one space, and an ID someone else used answers 409 `id_taken`. When an Owner deletes their account, the longest-standing Admin, else the longest-standing Member, becomes Owner, ordered by when each joined, then by account ID. A space with no one else in it keeps its row, its name and about text are erased, and `deleted_at` is set. Their other memberships end, `created_by` is credited to `deleted_account`, and their ID is scrubbed from `space_change`. Spaces are handed over before boards, so M3d-18 can find a space's new Owner. Download your data gains `spaces`, which lists each space, your role, and when you joined. It includes the name and about text only of a space you made. |
| Who sees what | Only a space's members learn that it exists. A non-member, a removed member, and anyone asking about a deleted, unknown, or malformed space get 404 `{reason: "not_found"}` on every read and write. Signed out gets 401 first. A Member's invite is refused with 403 before any username is looked up, so the space can't be used to try usernames. The member list shows usernames, never emails, and leaves out deleted accounts. It lists the Owner first, then Admins, then Members, each by username. Nothing lists a space you aren't in, and nothing lets anyone join one. A rename's change row says only which fields changed, and the database's own check refuses a row that holds a sentence. No server event is sent for a space write, so nothing new reaches PostHog. |
| The third table | The row asks that each space change be a change row that holds no text. The board change log, `change`, needs a board and that board's change number, and a space has neither. So `space_change` holds a space's changes, with an identity ID, `space_id`, `at`, `actor_id`, `kind`, and `before` and `after`, which use the board log's `holds_only_references` check. The service may only add rows to it, and the deletion role may only rewrite the actor and scrub `before` and `after`. |
| Limits | New spaces count toward the same 20 an hour as new boards (`limit: "board"`), so the limits table needs no new row. Space invitations count toward the same 20 an hour as board invitations, by hand in the route and whatever the answer, as the board route counts them. Renaming a space counts as a write, as renaming a board's question does. Visibility isn't counted, as Share isn't. |
| Rule 13 | The privacy sentence is "Everyone in a space sees the usernames of its other members and how many members it has. No one outside the space sees either." The refusal's screen text, "The owner of a space keeps that role.", is M3d-19's to show, and the service answers only the reason. |
| Checked by machine | New files pass: `app/shared/test/policy-spaces.test.ts` (8), `app/server/test/database/spaces.test.ts` (8), `app/server/test/api/spaces.test.ts` (39), `app/server/test/api/space-roles.test.ts` (80, that is 8 actions × 10 personas), and `app/server/test/database/spaces-service-login.test.ts` (1). The database tests cover the foreign keys and not-null columns, one membership per person per space, no length or value checks, rows that can't be removed, the no-text check, the grants of both service roles, and the upgrade from 0015 with boards and accounts unchanged. The API tests cover making a space as any signed-in account, signed out refused, "public" refused, a 41-character name and a 121-character about text refused while 40 and 120 are accepted, making a space twice, someone else's ID, and the limit on making spaces. They also cover reading as a member and 404 for a non-member, an unknown, a deleted, and a malformed space, plus 401 signed out, the member list's order, and your spaces, which leave out a deleted space you own. Settings by an Admin and the Owner are covered with a change row that holds no text, as are a Member refused, visibility for the Owner only, and invites with capitals and @, unknown and deleted names, the Owner, a Member refused before the lookup, a non-member, and the role kept or changed. The tests also cover the invite limit shared with board invites, role changes both ways, an Admin demoting and promoting another Admin, demoting the Owner refused, removal taking effect at once, an Admin removed, the Owner kept, leaving, each write sent twice, the three hand-over cases, a member's deletion, the deleted ID found nowhere but `account.id` in three scenarios, and the export, which leaves out a space you left and a deleted one. The role matrix also checks that a refused write leaves the space, its members, and its change rows as they were. The service-login test runs every route, the export, and the Owner's account deletion using only the rights the migrations grant, and checks that the Owner's space with no one else in it is deleted with its text erased. Unchanged files pass: all of `app/server/test/database` except the protected test below, plus `account.test.ts`, `comments-deletion.test.ts`, `invites.test.ts`, `rate-limits.test.ts`, `roles.test.ts`, `role-matrix.test.ts`, `people.test.ts`, `profiles.test.ts`, `bad-requests.test.ts`, `new-board.test.ts`, `username-change.test.ts`, `source-unavailable.test.ts`, `history-additions.test.ts`, `change-email.test.ts`, `reports.test.ts`, `history.test.ts`, `combine.test.ts`, `closing.test.ts`, `statistics.test.ts`, the simulation's `simulation.test.ts` and `profiles.test.ts`, every shared and web unit file, and the tooling guards. That is 1,545 tests in the first run and 114 in the second. `npm run typecheck` and eslint on the changed files pass. No browser spec was run, because the row adds no screen. |
| Tests first | All five new files were written before the build and run against it: all 135 tests failed, the shared ones because the exports didn't exist and the server ones because the tables didn't exist, which also failed the signed-out matrix cases in their setup. After the build, two of my own expectations were corrected. Leaving twice answers 404, not `already_done`, because someone who has left is no longer a member, and anything else would tell an outsider the space exists. The invite-limit test now uses the board's Owner, Ana, rather than moving a board to a new account. One type error was fixed: a test of a role word the database doesn't check now inserts it by SQL. |
| Fails, protected | `app/server/test/database/schema-shape.test.ts`, "M-01: every table the plan lists exists", lists every table exactly, so it fails once 0016 adds three. The line would become `expect(tables.rows.map((row) => row.tablename)).toEqual(["account", "board", "board_member", "space", "space_change", "space_member", "username_hold", ...BOARD_TABLES].sort());`. The space tables don't belong in `BOARD_TABLES` or `STAMPED_TABLES`, because they have no `board_id` or `changed_seq`. Rule 9 leaves it to the builder. The two failures already on this stack weren't run. |
| Products compared (rule 12) | The Slack point on demoting an Admin was checked live, and the rest come from memory. From memory, a Slack workspace has one Primary Owner, who can't be removed or deactivated and must transfer ownership to leave, plus Admins and Members. From memory, a GitHub organization has Owners and Members, an Owner may change any member's role, and an organization can't lose its last Owner. From memory, a Discord server has one owner, who can't be kicked and hands the server over by hand. Steelman copies the one Owner, who keeps that role, from Slack, and takes Admin and Member from Slack's names. Slack's live help page on changing a member's role, checked November 26, shows that only Workspace Owners and the Primary Owner demote a Workspace Admin, and Workspace Admins can't. A GitHub organization has no role between Owner and Member. Steelman follows the row and lets an Admin remove or demote another Admin, which goes further than Slack does. |
| Needs the builder | Add the three tables to `schema-shape.test.ts` as above, and approve the third table, `space_change`, which the row and architecture §2 don't name. Approve the space actions added to `policy.ts` with the `builder-approved` label. Confirm the privacy sentence. The page's date stays November 25, because `statistics.spec.ts:118` and `comments-account.spec.ts:19` hold it, and moving it would change both lines to `getByText("Updated November 26, 2026")`. Decide whether new spaces should share the new-board count, as now, or get their own 20 an hour, which would add a row to the limits table. Decide whether a partial unique index should hold "exactly one owner" in the database. Today the service holds it under the space's row lock, because Q24 keeps role words out of the schema. Decide whether an Admin may remove or demote another Admin, which the row allows and Slack doesn't, as the comparison above shows. Decide whether a member's export should also carry the space's name, which it now carries only for a space they made. Moving G-03 and G-04 to T3 was already done in `features.csv`. |
| The builder's approval | On Nov 26 the builder approved `space`, `space_change` and `space_member` joining `schema-shape.test.ts`'s list of tables, the space actions added to `policy.ts` below the board table, the third table `space_change`, and the privacy page's sentence about what a space's members see. |
| Not checked | Staging (rule 14) is checked after the merge deploys. No screen exists yet, so nothing was checked in a browser. Two deletions racing for one space were reasoned about (both lock spaces in ID order) but not run together. |

## November 26 — Boards in a space: Everyone in the space (G-04, G-05, H-02, M-07)

Build plan M3d-18, not marked done: the `access.ts` change, the account-deletion role's new rights, and a protected database test that this migration breaks wait for the builder. A new migration, `server/migrations/0017_board_spaces.sql`, adds a nullable `board.space_id` that references `space`, an index on it, and lets the account-deletion role add a board member and set `joined_via`. `server/src/db/database.ts` gains the column. `shared/src/api.ts` widens `accessInput` with "space", adds `boardSpaceInput`, refuses "space" on a new board, and adds `Standing.space`, two History details (`spaceName` and `outOfSpace`), and the refusal `no_space`. `server/src/access.ts` gains the space path in `entryTo`, `spaceBoards()` for the list, profiles, and statistics, and `spaceShownTo()`. `server/src/sharing.ts` changes `setAccess` and adds `setBoardSpace`. `server/src/app.ts` adds `PUT /api/boards/:board/space` and sends `space` with the board and its changes. `server/src/list.ts`, `profiles.ts`, and `statistics.ts` add the space's boards. `server/src/history.ts` names the space to its members. `server/src/account.ts` hands a space board over and adds `space` to the export. On the web, `web/src/board/spaceAccess.ts` holds the words, and `Sharing.tsx`, `History.tsx`, `session.ts`, and `Board.tsx` use them. `policy.ts` is unchanged.

| Check | Observed result |
|---|---|
| What was built | The board's Owner puts it in a space they belong to with `PUT /api/boards/:board/space {space}`, or takes it out with `{space: null}`. Both are Share's (`POLICY.share`), so only the Owner may, and not on a closed board. A space the Owner isn't in, a deleted one, and one that doesn't exist all answer 409 `no_space`. General access "space" means everyone in the board's space at the link's role. A board in no space, in a deleted one, or in one its Owner has left or was removed from refuses it with 409 `no_space`, as putting the board in a space does. A board still shared with a space that was deleted, because its last member deleted their account, reads as invited only, since no one gets in through that space. Its new Owner is never told that everyone in the space can open it. Switching to the space removes the people who came by the link, as turning the link off does. Moving a shared board out of its space, or into another, sets it to invited only in the same write, so it never opens to a group the Owner didn't choose. Each write adds one change row, `space_changed` or `access_changed`, which holds the space's ID and never its name. `entryTo` reads the person's board role first. Failing that, a member of a space that isn't deleted gets the link's role on a board shared with it. That role is read from `space_member` on every request and never recorded, so leaving or removal ends it at once, and a board role of their own stays. `listBoards`, profiles, and statistics add the same boards through `spaceBoards()`, which leaves out a board the person has a role on, so it is listed once, at their own role. A board in a space that isn't shared with it opens only as its own access says, by the link included. When a space board's Owner deletes their account, the board passes to the space's Owner, else its longest-standing Admin, who is added or promoted as Owner by name. Their spaces were handed over first, so this is the new Owner. With the space deleted for lack of members, the board falls back to its own editors and contributors as before. Download your data adds `space` to each board in a space the person is in. |
| Who sees what | Someone outside the space with no board role gets 403 `no_access` on the board, its changes, its people, and its History, as on an invited-only board, and never sees it in their list, a profile, or statistics. The board's answers carry `space: {id, name}` only for a reader who is in the space, so an ordinary answer keeps its shape. History gives `spaceName` only to such a reader. Anyone else on the board reads "Everyone in the space". Share's people list shows the board's recorded people. A reader let in through the space also sees themself, after the Owner, at the space's role and marked "(you)", as the prototype always lists you. No one else in the space is listed, since only a space's members see its member list. The space's name in the pill, the General access menu, the hint, and History sits in `ph-no-capture`. The board's read-out region, `#sm-status`, which names the space and people after a change, is in `ph-no-capture` too. |
| Share | General access lists "Invited only", "Everyone in POD 6", and "Anyone with the link", in the prototype's order. The role menu shows beside the space choice too and is named "Space role" there, while "Link role" keeps its name for the link. The pill and the access icon use the prototype's `users` icon. The hint is "Everyone in POD 6 can find this steelman and vote.", and for Viewer it is "… can find this steelman and read it.", and for Contributor "… can find this steelman, vote, and write." Each is followed by "Anyone who can open it can comment." After a change, the board reads out "Everyone in POD 6 can vote." On a board in no space, the choice reads "Everyone in a space" and can't be chosen, as the prototype greys it. Removing a member from a space board reads out "lin.zhao no longer has access. lin.zhao can still open this steelman if they’re in POD 6." `web/test/space-access-sentences.test.ts` produces that sentence, the refusal below, and History's line for taking a board out. |
| History | A member of the space reads "maya.chen let everyone in POD 6 vote." and "maya.chen put the board in POD 6." Anyone else reads "maya.chen let everyone in the space vote." and "maya.chen put the board in a space." Taking a shared board out reads "maya.chen took the board out of its space, so only invited people can open it." |
| Rule 13 | Every new line above is a full sentence in plain present tense. The labels are short: "Everyone in POD 6", "Everyone in a space", and "Space role". The refusal reads "This steelman isn’t in a space you’re in, so it can’t be shared with everyone in one.", which also holds for an Owner who has left the space. |
| Checked by machine | New files pass: `app/shared/test/space-access.test.ts` (3), `app/web/test/space-access.test.ts` (4), `app/server/test/api/space-access-matrix.test.ts` (52, that is 5 actions × 10 personas, plus a closed board and each write sent twice), `app/server/test/api/space-boards.test.ts` (33), `app/server/test/database/board-spaces.test.ts` (4), the simulation's `space-access.test.ts` (1, 30 random sequences) and `planted-space-access.test.ts` (3), each run alone, `app/web/test/space-access-sentences.test.ts` (3), and `app/e2e/space-access.spec.ts` (8, at 1280 and 375 px, with the accessibility scan passing with Share open and with History open). The simulation now checks each step's answer and lowercases the username it invites, as the route does, so its invite and remove steps take effect and a step that quietly does nothing fails the run. Each planted test has a 60-second limit. Unchanged files pass: all of `app/server/test/database` except the protected test below, plus `account.test.ts`, `role-matrix.test.ts`, `roles.test.ts`, `space-roles.test.ts`, `spaces.test.ts`, `profiles.test.ts`, `statistics.test.ts`, `list.test.ts`, `list-comment-counts.test.ts`, `list-comments.test.ts`, `new-board.test.ts`, `reading.test.ts`, `comments-reading.test.ts`, `history.test.ts`, `history-additions.test.ts`, `people.test.ts`, `invites.test.ts`, `bad-requests.test.ts`, `comments-deletion.test.ts`, `example-boards.test.ts`, and `stale-writes.test.ts` (672 of 673). The simulation's `simulation.test.ts`, `profiles.test.ts`, and `planted-profiles.test.ts` pass, each run alone. The first test in `planted-profiles.test.ts` once hit its 5-second limit under the machine's load, then took 0.9 s on two reruns, against 1.06 s without this change. Every web and shared unit file passes (304). The browser specs `sharing`, `people-list`, `invite`, `closed-board`, `history`, `share-and-axis`, and `new-steelman` pass, saved screenshots included. `npm run typecheck` and eslint on the changed files pass. After the review's fixes, the new files, the related API and database files, both space simulation files run alone, every web unit file (155), the browser specs `space-access`, `sharing`, `people-list`, `invite`, `share-and-axis`, and `share-open`, the type check, and eslint were run again. Each passed except the two protected failures below. |

The tests added after the review (the people list, an Owner who has left the space, a deleted space's access, the sentences, and the read-out region) were written with their fixes and weren't seen failing first. |

From memory, Drive's own menu puts the organisation between the other two, which the prototype's order follows. ## Limits

- No participant comprehension study or retention evidence.
- No claim of full screen-reader, cross-browser, or device coverage.
- Standalone state persists in browser local storage when available; it does not sync with other users or devices. Fonts and icons need internet access.
- Material revision history and shared collaboration are not implemented. Presence, other viewers, spaces, and invitations are simulated.
- Live changes from other people are simulated (Simulate others); they don't validate an aggregation algorithm or a realtime backend.

## Re-run manually

Serve `docs/`, open `/prototype/`, and follow the workflow in `README.md`. Check changing and withdrawing all three judgments, unfolding and folding a stack in place, empty suggestion validation, adding a local version, Escape order, unavailable-source behavior, explicit order updates, and local-state restoration on reload. Check the Product Page at wide and narrow widths, including anchor navigation, image loading, and prototype/source links.
